# docs/IMPLEMENTATION_LOG.md

**Purpose:** the single place where every decision and every piece of implementation work is recorded, by humans and AI agents. Bugs and errors go in `docs/BUG_LOG.md`, not here (link between them by ID).

## Rules
1. **Append-only.** Add new entries at the **top of the Entries section** (newest first). Never edit or delete an old entry. To correct one, add a new entry that says `Supersedes: IMPL-0007` / `Amends: DEC-0003`.
2. **One entry per task or decision**, written when the work is finished (or when the decision is made), before you end your session.
3. **Truthful.** Status words only: Verified, Implemented, Tested, Partial, Proposed, Unverified, Blocked, Deprecated. Paste real command output summaries; never write "tests pass" without counts. Say "not run" when you did not run it.
4. **No secrets, tokens, real personal data or document contents.** Refer to files by path and hash.
5. Decisions that need a human (see Essentials: approval gates) are logged as `Proposed` with `Owner: <human>` until approved; do not implement them first.
6. IDs: `DEC-0001…` decisions, `IMPL-0001…` implementation entries, `BUG-0001…` live in the bug log. Use the next unused number.

## Entry template: decision
```
### DEC-XXXX — <short title>
- Date / author (human or agent name + model):
- Status: Proposed | Accepted | Rejected | Superseded by DEC-XXXX
- Context (what forced the decision; link PRD/FR/OPEN IDs and sources):
- Options considered (A/B/C with one-line pros/cons):
- Decision:
- Rationale:
- Consequences (what it changes, what it blocks, follow-ups):
- Approval needed from / approved by / date:
- Evidence (links to commits, tests, official source IDs):
```

## Entry template: implementation
```
### IMPL-XXXX — <short title>
- Date / agent (name + model) / branch / commit hash:
- Task ID (AGENT-xx or plan item) and PRD IDs (FR-xx):
- Goal:
- What existed before (files/symbols reused, or "search found nothing"):
- What I did (bullet list; plain language):
- Files changed (path — reason):
- Migrations / API / config / rule-file changes:
- Decisions made (link DEC-XXXX; any new decision must also get its own DEC entry):
- Permission and audit effects:
- Verification: exact commands + results (counts, skips and why). Anything not run = "not run".
- Status per requirement touched (status words):
- Limitations / assumptions / manual steps:
- Related bugs (BUG-XXXX):
- Open questions for a human:
- Next suggested task:
```

## Entries (newest first)

### DEC-0013 — Public EoA source pointer and fictional regression fixture
The PRD's public EoA demo source is recorded as a source pointer, but the real institution PDF and its data are not copied into the repository because `AGENTS.md` prohibits committing real institution documents. The executable master-record JSON is fictional, labelled synthetic, and test-only. Synthetic Format-2-style NOC and Format-3-style Resolution JSON fixtures exercise existing classification and advisory cross-check/site-check services. Official form fields and regulatory interpretations remain Unverified; fixtures do not establish compliance or authority.

### IMPL-0049 — Phase 5.10 synthetic site-move fixtures (FR-38, FR-42, FR-44)
- Task / PRD IDs: Phase 5.10, FR-38, FR-42, FR-44.
- What existed: classifier and advisory E01–E03 / SITE-01/02 services existed, but no joined labelled fixture pack tied them together. The repo already contained a synthetic DOCX NOC example; no Format-3 fixture was present.
- Work: added JSON-only fictional test fixtures for the EoA-shaped master-record values, Format-2-style NOC, and Format-3-style Resolution. Added a source pointer for the public demo EoA; did not download or copy the real institution PDF/data. Added one contract test for class recognition, cross-document matches, full programme coverage and advisory-only outputs.
- Files changed: `backend/tests/fixtures/phase5_site_move/{README.md,eoa_master_record.json,format_2_noc.json,format_3_resolution.json}`, `backend/tests/test_phase5_site_move_fixtures.py`, `docs/SOURCES.md`, `docs/DECISIONS.md`, `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`, `CONTEXT.md`, `README.md`, `docs/BUG_LOG.md`.
- Existing code reused: `doc_classifier.classify`, `evidence_crosschecks.evaluate`, `site_checks.evaluate`, and `Snapshot`.
- API/schema/config: none; no migration or dependency change.
- Permission/audit effects: none; no persisted application state or audit events.
- Verification: `../.venv/bin/pytest -q tests/test_phase5_site_move_fixtures.py` → **1 passed, 1 upstream warning**; `make lint` → clean; `make test` → **777 passed, 1 upstream warning, 0 skipped**. Initial bare system pytest failed before collection from an unrelated global plugin (BUG-0042); initial lint flagged test import order, fixed (BUG-0043).
- Limitations: fixture values are wholly fictional; official Format-2/Format-3 templates and field mappings are not verified. Source pointer is not an integrated live/public data feed. No UI/demo auto-fill or split-screen work is claimed here; planned next task is 5.11.
- Open questions: PRD §19 template/field mapping questions remain unresolved; no regulatory rule status changed.
- Git status at implementation: pending final commit and push; unrelated untracked image files were left untouched.

### IMPL-0048 — Phase 5.9: optional OpenRouter vision fallback (FR-39, FR-51)
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-27 / Codex / master / pending commit.
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 5.9 (AGENT-04); FR-39 and FR-51; PRD §8 lines 134/161.
- Goal: provide a config-driven vision adapter for low-confidence pages, strict structured output, grounding, an off switch, and a metadata log for every provider attempt.
- What existed before: `OCRExtractor` + grounding service; Gemini-backed lease parser was separate. No OpenRouter adapter, config, or `ExternalCallLog` existed.
- What I did:
  - Added `Settings.openrouter_*`: disabled by default, model configurable, confidence threshold default 0.75 and range checked, timeout bounded; a key alone never enables use.
  - Added `adapters/vision/openrouter.py` using the official Chat Completions multimodal endpoint, `temperature=0`, strict `json_schema`, and `provider.require_parameters`. Local validation rejects malformed/extra fields, invalid confidence/page numbers, and duplicate field names.
  - Every candidate is grounded against the OCR page text through the existing fuzzy grounding service; ungrounded candidates remain `REVIEW_REQUIRED`. The adapter only suggests fields and its prompt prohibits compliance/workflow decisions.
  - External calls require an internal caller to assert trusted public/synthetic provenance and page confidence below threshold. The public upload API does not expose that assertion or invoke this adapter, preventing ordinary institution documents from being sent.
  - Added `ExternalCallLog` + migration `0017_external_call_logs`. ### DEC-0010 — Outcome labels never create a formal finding or gate
- Date / author: 2026-10-27 / Codex
- Status: Accepted
- Context: FR-46 asks for three pre-check labels and explicitly says none blocks; E01–E03 and SITE-01/02 remain Unverified pending official source mappings.
- Options considered: treat a comparison signal as a formal deficiency; show a candidate label while retaining human review; suppress the three aggregate outcomes until rules are Verified.
- Decision: expose the PRD's labels as nonbinding review guidance. A discrepancy may be called `DEFICIENCY CANDIDATE`, but no Deficiency record is created. Missing/Unverified checks result in `REVIEW REQUIRED`. `PRE-CHECK PASSED` requires all applicable mappings Verified and complete.
- Rationale: matches FR-46 while preserving human decision authority and the explicit blocked/Unverified status of the placeholder rules.
- Consequences: result always has `blocking: false`, `creates_deficiency: false`, and `automatic_workflow_action: false`; an officer remains responsible for every formal action.
- Approval needed from / approved by / date: none; this adds no workflow state, permission, or authority.
- Evidence: `backend/tests/test_precheck_status.py`; linked IMPL-0046.

### DEC-0007 — How a claim is stored, gated and evaluated
- Date / author: 2026-10-27 / MiMo (OpenCode agent)
- Status: Accepted
- Context: FR-41 keeps Extraction/Claim/Master per field. Where does the claim live, who may write it, and what does it affect?
- Options considered: (A) overwrite `value` and keep a history row — violates PRD line 140 (*"both values kept"*) and RULE-EDIT-01 (*"Do not silently overwrite"*); (B) store claim beside extraction, gate `require_any(DOCUMENT_UPLOAD, DOCUMENT_VERIFY)`, do not bump `baseline_version`, no master column; (C) add a `MASTER` role permission + master column with placeholder data — invents an authority and a source the PRD does not have (spec: *"if one is actually integrated"*).
- Decision: (B). Four nullable columns beside `value`; edits require a reason (400 before anything is recorded); claims never bump `baseline_version` (rules read the Extraction Value only — pinned by a snapshot-equality test); master slot answers `None` + the spec's caveat; `require_any` so no `ROLE_PERMISSIONS` change and no approval-gate side effect.
- Rationale: PRD line 140 and RULE-EDIT-01 mandate separation and provenance, never mutation; DEC-0003 forbids new gating; an absent source must stay unknown rather than guessed.
- Consequences: `FieldOut.master_value` is always `null` today; RULE-EDIT-01 answers `REVIEW REQUIRED` only for broken provenance paths (the API refuses to create one); officers still decide what a value means.
- Approval needed from / approved by / date: none — no role authority, route, fee or deadline changed; noted for the human in IMPL-0043 open questions.
- Evidence: `backend/tests/test_three_value.py` (15), `tests/test_authz_matrix.py` MATRIX row; linked IMPL-0043.

### DEC-0006 — Record explicit audit checks with POST and keep exports scoped
- Date / author: 2026-10-27 / Codex
- Status: Accepted
- Context: The existing verification endpoint was a read-only GET. The detailed audit contract also calls for an auditable verification event, and the audit export must preserve object-level scope.
- Options considered: (A) make GET append a verification event, which changes safe-method semantics and logs on every refresh; (B) preserve GET and add explicit POST for a recorded check; (C) expose a full global event dump so a case export can be independently recomputed, which discloses unrelated cases.
- Decision: Keep GET side-effect-free; add POST `/audit/verify` to record actor, result, timestamp and request id. Export only events associated with the authorized application, while verifying the complete chain server-side and including the verified chain head.
- Rationale: explicit verification is auditable without turning reads into writes, and case scoping preserves authorization. The export states that it is not independently verifiable without other applications' event payloads.
- Consequences: timeline UI has explicit “Verify and record check” and export actions. Global-chain verification remains server-side; independently verifiable per-case proofs would need a different cryptographic structure and are not claimed.
- Approval needed from / approved by / date: none; no regulatory or authority behavior changed.
- Evidence: `backend/tests/test_phase8_audit.py`, `scripts/tamper_demo.py`; linked IMPL-0042.

### IMPL-0042 — Phase 8 audit coverage, timeline export and tamper demonstration
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-27 / Codex / `master` / uncommitted at log time
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 8.1–8.3, exit check; PRD §12 / `md/rules.json` audit event contract; FR-12 assignment audit. Phase 8.1 remains Partial for capabilities not yet present (rule/config edit and appeal mutation flows).
- Goal: Complete audit evidence for the sensitive and material operations already supported, deliver a scoped case export, and prove clean/tampered chain behavior on a throwaway database.
- What existed before: SHA-256 audit service and chain head (`services/audit_service.py`); `/applications/{id}/audit` scoped, paginated endpoint; `/audit/verify` read-only endpoint; `AuditTimeline.tsx`. Document content reads and extraction lifecycle lacked events; compliance runs and automatic case hand-offs lacked audit records; no export or tamper demo existed.
- What I did:
  - Record successful document content access without file contents; persist extraction start before work, and record completed/failed outcomes with bounded metadata.
  - Record `document.replaced` for subsequent versions, `finding.batch_evaluated` for explicit and stale-batch evaluations, and `assignment.created` in the same workflow transaction as the hand-off.
  - Add `POST /audit/verify` for an actor-attributed audit verification event while leaving GET read-only.
  - Add `GET /applications/{id}/audit/export`: enforce existing `AUDIT_READ` plus application scope, verify the full chain before export, write `audit.exported`, verify again, then return only that case's events and the checked chain head. The bundle states that scoped payloads cannot independently recompute the global chain.
  - Update the timeline UI with truthful tamper-evident wording, explicit verification/export controls, and errors; preserve the existing endpoint and component.
  - Add `scripts/tamper_demo.py` using a temporary SQLite database; it verifies two clean events, mutates a throwaway event, and confirms detection.

### DEC-0005 — Execution order: Phase 6 and Phase 8 next; Phase 5.4–5.11 remain open
- Date / author / status: 2026-10-27 / reyyishreyas (human) + MiMo agent / **Accepted** (human instruction in the working session: *"follow it completely to finish up the phase 6 and phase 8 of the implementation plan"*).
- Context: the plan's rule (plan line 3) says tasks run top-to-bottom and a phase starts only after the previous phase's exit check. Phase 5 is complete through **5.3**; **5.4–5.11 and the Phase 5 exit check are unfinished**, so starting Phase 6 departs from the written order.
- Options considered: (1) continue Phase 5.4 in order; (2) run Phase 6 + Phase 8 as instructed, then return to Phase 5.4–5.11; (3) run both concurrently (rejected — `4_AGENTS.md` forbids concurrent phases without agreed interfaces).
- Decision: **(2)** — the project lead explicitly directed Phase 6 and Phase 8. Phase 5.4–5.11 stay unticked and are carried as open work; nothing in Phases 6/8 depends on 5.4–5.11 (no three-value model, no E01–E03/SITE rules, no split-screen UI are required by FR-18–FR-27/FR-31–FR-32 or by Phase 8's audit items).
- Rationale: the human approver may re-order the plan; the rule exists to stop *agents* from skipping phases on their own.
- Consequences: plan phase order shows Phase 6/8 complete (or in progress) while Phase 5 is partially open — each tick records the real state; the Phase 5 exit check remains a prerequisite for calling Phase 5 done; the Phase 6 exit check still runs at the end of Phase 6.
- Who must approve: already approved by the project lead (this session). Superseded-by-human only if the lead changes the order again.

### IMPL-0041 — Phase 6.1: EVC queue entry after scrutiny when the route needs it (FR-18)
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-27 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / feature commit `425e211`, gates green at that SHA (docs commit follows).
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 6 **6.1** (plan line 94: *"`EVC` model; queue entry after scrutiny when the route needs it (FR-18)"*); PRD **FR-18** (line 192: *"Queue for EVC after scrutiny when the route needs it"*, APH col. `2.23.2` per the PRD table — clause mapping Unverified). AGENT-09/AGENT-08. Also touched DEC-0005 (phase order).
- Goal: the EVC coordinator's queue must show a case the moment its route says an expert visit is due — not only after someone remembers to book one. Until now the queue held only `evc_scheduled`/`evc_completed` cases and cases with a live booking, so the whole "waiting to be scheduled" population was invisible (exactly the population FR-18 is about).
- What existed before: `EvcVisit` model (migration 0008) with scheduling/reschedule/completion bookkeeping; `GET /evc/queue` (`api/evc.py`) covering `evc_scheduled`/`evc_completed` + any case with a `scheduled` visit, scoped by `scope_filter`, `MONITORING_READ`-gated with a matrix row; booking via `POST .../evc/visits` with the `schedule_evc` auto-transition (existing, covered by `test_routes_and_revert.py`); no dedicated EVC test file.
- What I did (tests first — `backend/tests/test_evc_queue.py`, **11 tests, all ran, none skipped**):
  - **Queue selection** — `evc_queue` additionally reads the route's *own* transition definitions (`WorkflowTransitionDefinition` where `action == "schedule_evc"`) into `(route_key, from_state)` pairs and ORs `Application.route_key == rk AND current_state == fs` into the selection, plus cases already holding a live visit or sitting at an EVC state. "Route needs EVC" therefore comes from configuration the route already declares — never from the category and never guessed. A route without an EVC stage (`existing_technical`) contributes no pair; a case before its eligible state (submitted on `new_technical`, draft) does not match.
  - **Row flag** — each row gains `awaiting_scheduling: bool` (matched the route pair **and** holds no live visit); the payload gains an `awaiting_scheduling` count next to the existing `not_scheduled`/`visit_due_soon`/`visit_overdue` counts (all additive keys).
  - **Derived on read** — no table, no stored queue row, no state change; reading the queue writes nothing (asserted by a row-count + state snapshot test).
- Files changed: `backend/app/api/evc.py` (selection clause, row flag, count, docstring), `backend/tests/test_evc_queue.py` (new, 11), `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (6.1 ticked), `CONTEXT.md`, `README.md`, this log.
- Existing code reused: `EvcVisit` (model untouched), `scope_filter`/`EffectiveAccess`, `require(MONITORING_READ)` + the existing authz-matrix row, `_serialise(visit, …)`, `stage_sla_days`, the booking auto-transition path, `WorkflowTransitionDefinition` (the same table `_route_states` already reads).
- API / schema / config / migration changes: **no new route, no migration, no new config file** — `GET /evc/queue` response gains one boolean per row and one count (additive); no permission matrix changes.
- Permission and audit effects: **none** — reads only, through the existing `MONITORING_READ` gate and the caller's scope; no audit events (nothing changes); no transition is offered or implied by the queue.
- Decisions made (extensions of DEC-0003; no new DEC id): (1) **Queue membership is derived, never stored** — consistent with the repo's computed-on-read reports; a stored queue row would need invalidation on revert/reschedule for no benefit. (2) **Eligibility = route's own `schedule_evc` from-state**, so `evc_only` correctly enters from `submitted` (that route has no scrutiny stage) while `new_technical` enters from `scrutiny`. (3) A booked-but-still-in-scrutiny case shows the appointment and `awaiting_scheduling: false` — the booking, not the state, ends "awaiting". (4) The queue stays advisory: it never marks scrutiny complete (that is the `schedule_evc` transition and its guards).
- Verification commands and results:
  - `pytest tests/test_evc_queue.py -q` → first run **1 failed, 9 passed** (the booking test wrongly assumed the seeded `new_technical` case clears `schedule_evc`'s rule/document guards — it does not, so the gate correctly held; no product bug). Rewritten to assert the gate-held path (booking stands, appointment kept) plus a separate `evc_only` walk covering the gated-advance path → **11 passed, 0 skipped**.
  - `make lint` → "All checks passed!"
  - `make test` → **725 passed, 3 skipped** in 61s (up from 714; +11). Skips: the 3 pre-existing Tesseract-marked tests (`test_document_extraction` ×2, `test_field_provenance` ×1) — **this machine currently has no `tesseract` binary** (see environment note below).
- Environment note: at session start `pytest -q` reported **714 passed, 3 skipped** with "Tesseract is not installed on this machine" — i.e. this session runs **without** Tesseract (IMPL-0039's 27 Oct note recorded it present; the machine state changed between sessions). BUG-0001 stays `Workaround`; the 3 skips are pre-existing and unrelated to this change.
- Limitations: (a) **"After scrutiny" is operationalised as "at the state the route schedules from"** — the queue cannot know a scrutiny result is recorded (Phase 4.3's overall result) without coupling to that feature; a case mid-scrutiny on an EVC route appears as `awaiting_scheduling` while the `schedule_evc` guards still hold the line (advisory row, no action offered). (b) `evc_only` cases appear from `submitted` by the same rule (their route has no scrutiny) — correct for the route, but the coordinator sees them immediately after submission. (c) The queue still carries no explicit "random EVC" (FR-19) marker — additional-EVC actions remain the advisory `routing.additional_evc_actions()` lookup from Phase 1.6. (d) No frontend change — `EvcPanel.tsx` renders whatever rows the API returns; `npm run build` not run (no UI change).
- Open questions for a human: should a case whose Phase 4.3 overall scrutiny result is recorded (`nil_deficiency`) be the trigger instead of / in addition to the route-state rule? Should `awaiting_scheduling` rows carry an explicit "eligible now vs. guards not met" hint (would require re-evaluating `schedule_evc` guards per row)?
- Next suggested task: **Phase 6.2** (plan line 95) — schedule online/physical, experts, confirmation, Region calendar (FR-20); reminders 7/2/1 days. *(Overall order per DEC-0005: continue Phase 6, then Phase 8.)*

### IMPL-0040 — Phase 5.3: `fuzzy_grounding.py` — model values must be found on the page (FR-40)
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-27 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / (this commit; gates green at this SHA)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 5 **5.3** (plan line 82: *"`fuzzy_grounding.py`: model value must match OCR words on page else `REVIEW_REQUIRED` (FR-40)"*); PRD **FR-40** (line 224) and §8 step 3 (line 135: *"a model value counts only if its text is found on the page (fuzzy match to OCR words); else `REVIEW_REQUIRED`"*); **Essentials rule 10** (vision-model values count only if grounded); the config cites **RULE-OCR-01** (`md/rules.json`) — its "value not found on page" leg. AGENT-04.
- Goal: an LLM can produce a plausible, confident value that is simply not in the document. Only the page can vouch for it, and the page is read by OCR (which misreads characters) — so the check is a *fuzzy* match of the model value against the page's words: found ⇒ `grounded`, not found ⇒ `REVIEW_REQUIRED` for a human. Never a rejection, never a mutation, never a guessed pass.
- What existed before: no `fuzzy_grounding` anywhere (service, config or vocabulary); `ExtractedField` had no grounding column (PRD §8 line 159 lists "grounding result" as per-field data); `page_texts` already existed from 5.1 (the real reader and grounded extractor populate it) — the page words were available, nothing compared values to them; the only model-produced field values are the Gemini path (`method="gemini_grounded_ai"`, off by default — no key, external calls disabled per AGENTS); `RULE-OCR-01` exists only in the spec `md/rules.json`, not in `backend/rules/`.
- What I did (tests first — `backend/tests/test_fuzzy_grounding.py`, **13 tests, all ran, none skipped**):
  - **Config** — `backend/rules/fuzzy_grounding.json` (`grounding_id RULE-OCR-GROUND`, **status `proposed_unverified`**, `advisory_only`, source = FR-40 + §8 line 135 + Essentials 10, `rule_reference RULE-OCR-01`): `model_methods: ["gemini_grounded_ai"]` (which methods count as model values is config, not code), `matching {case_sensitive:false, min_token_length:3, token_similarity:0.85, min_match_ratio:1.0}` with a **note declaring the thresholds labelled placeholders** (the PRD requires fuzzy matching but gives no numbers and §19 does not list them — so they are set, marked, and await human calibration), and the status vocabulary `grounded` / `REVIEW_REQUIRED` taken from the spec, not invented.
  - **Service** — `services/fuzzy_grounding.py`: `config()` (policy loader), `ground(value=, page_texts=, policy=) -> dict` and `ground_candidates(candidates, page_texts) -> list[str]` (warnings). Matching: NFKC normalise + casefold (per config) + punctuation to spaces; significant tokens = length ≥ `min_token_length`; each token must be on a page — exact set lookup first, else `SequenceMatcher` best-ratio ≥ `token_similarity` (the fuzzy leg: one misread character is forgiven, absence is not); `grounded` when the matched fraction ≥ `min_match_ratio` (default 1.0 — conservative: *all* significant tokens must be found). Returns `grounded`, `status`, `similarity` (mean best ratio), `matched`/`tokens` counts and a human-readable `reason` (names the missing tokens, or `no_page_text` / `empty_value` / `no_significant_tokens`). Dict-shaped Gemini values (`{value, source_quote, ...}`) are grounded by their inner `value` (the quote is the citation, not the claim).
  - **Honest failures** — no page text, an empty value, or a value too short to check (`"12"`) all answer `REVIEW_REQUIRED`, never a pass (Essentials rule 16: missing input is never a guessed pass).
  - **Wiring** — `documents.py`, right after 5.1's classification: `fuzzy_grounding.ground_candidates(extraction.fields, extraction.page_texts)` appends its warnings to `extraction.warnings` (the existing channel the UI already renders — **no frontend change**), then the field loop persists `grounding=candidate.grounding`. Only configured model methods are touched; OCR/mock candidates keep `NULL` (they *are* the page's words — nothing to ground them against).
  - **Model + API** — `ExtractedField.grounding` (String(32) nullable) + `ExtractedFieldCandidate.grounding` + `FieldOut.grounding` (optional key, additive).
  - **Migration** — `migrations/versions/b2fade9a8767_extracted_field_grounding_result.py` (`down_revision f7e6eed3fa19`): one **nullable** `add_column` via `batch_alter_table`, additive only — no approval gate. Docstring explains what/why; `ruff --fix` run immediately after `alembic revision` (BUG-0019's lesson — the template imports failed `I001` again and were fixed before ever reaching `make lint`).
- Files changed: `backend/rules/fuzzy_grounding.json` (new), `backend/app/services/fuzzy_grounding.py` (new), `backend/app/services/policy.py` (+`fuzzy_grounding_policy()`), `backend/app/adapters/ocr/base.py` (+candidate key), `backend/app/models/application.py` (+column + docstring), `backend/app/api/documents.py` (wiring + persist + import), `backend/app/schemas/api.py` (`FieldOut`), `backend/migrations/versions/b2fade9a8767_*.py` (new), `backend/tests/test_fuzzy_grounding.py` (new, 13), `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (5.3 ticked), `CONTEXT.md`, `README.md`, this log, `docs/BUG_LOG.md`.
- Existing code reused: 5.1's **warnings-on-a-201 advisory pattern** (the grounding warning rides `extraction.warnings` exactly like the classifier's mismatch note — no new response shape), `page_texts` from 5.1, the `policy.py` `@lru_cache _load()` loader pattern, `FieldOut`'s optional-key style, the PRD-pinning test technique from 5.1 (parse `md/1_PRD.md`/Essentials so config and spec cannot drift).
- API / schema / config changes: **1 new config file** (`rules/fuzzy_grounding.json`); **1 new optional key** (`grounding`) on every field object in upload/list responses; **1 new migration**; no new routes → no permission matrix rows; `verification_status` vocabulary untouched (still `verified|rejected|unverified` — `REVIEW_REQUIRED` lives in the separate grounding field, deliberately not in the officer-owned status).
- Permission and audit effects: **none** — grounding is computed on upload under the existing `DOCUMENT_UPLOAD` flow and read through existing `DOCUMENT_READ` routes; no new audit events (no state change — the field's value/status are unchanged, only a provenance column and a warning are added); officer verification still rewrites only `verification_status` (tested: grounding survives verify). **No rule, transition or outcome reads `grounding`** — it is display/trust data; rules keep reading `verification_status` and degrade to `NEEDS_REVIEW` as before (DEC-0003 intact: nothing here approves, rejects, returns or blocks).
- Decisions made (extensions of DEC-0003; no new DEC id): (1) **Only configured model methods are grounded** — OCR values are the page's own words; adding the vision fallback later is a config line, not a code change. (2) **Honest failure states all map to `REVIEW_REQUIRED`** (`no_page_text`, `empty_value`, `no_significant_tokens`) — a check that cannot run is not a pass. (3) **Grounding never mutates anything the model could otherwise influence**: value, doc_type and every status are unchanged; the ungrounded value stays stored as a candidate (three-value model) with a warning for a human. (4) **Thresholds are placeholders, labelled in the config note** — the PRD gave no numbers and §19 does not ask about them, so they are configuration pending human calibration, not invented constants in code. (5) **`min_match_ratio` 1.0 (all significant tokens)** is the conservative reading of "its text is found on the page"; `token_similarity` 0.85 forgives a substitution. (6) **Similarity is computed but not persisted** — PRD line 159 says "grounding result", and the status is that result; the score is available to any caller at computation time (persisting it is listed as an open question).
- Verification commands and results:
  - `pytest tests/test_fuzzy_grounding.py -q` → **13 passed, 0 skipped** (config pinned to PRD §8/FR-40/Essentials 10; thresholds are placeholders; found ⇒ grounded; one OCR typo still found; not found ⇒ `REVIEW_REQUIRED` naming the token; no page text ⇒ review; `"12"` ⇒ review; case/punct/spacing; dict-shaped value; only model methods grounded + one warning; values/methods unchanged by grounding; column exists + seeded NULLs; upload 201 stores both statuses + warning, verify leaves grounding untouched)
  - First run after implementation: **2 failed** (case-insensitivity broken, warnings read from the wrong key) → **BUG-0020**, **BUG-0021**; re-run: **1 failed** (unscoped query hit seeded rows) → **BUG-0022**; all three Fixed with regression coverage in this suite.
  - `make lint` → "All checks passed!" (the `I001` in the generated migration was pre-empted with `ruff --fix` right after `alembic revision` — BUG-0019's follow-through).
  - `make test` → **717 passed** in 64s (up from 704; +13). **0 skipped.**
  - `SIH_DATABASE_URL=sqlite:////tmp/sih.db alembic upgrade head` → `f7e6eed3fa19 -> b2fade9a8767`; `alembic check` → "No new upgrade operations detected."
- Limitations: (a) **Token-based and Latin-oriented** — NFKC + `\W` tokenisation; a value written entirely in a script with no word separators could yield no significant tokens ⇒ `REVIEW_REQUIRED` (honest, but noisy for non-Latin documents — noted for a human). (b) **No index over page words** — each unmatched value token runs `SequenceMatcher` against every page word; fine for prototype page sizes, O(value×page) if ever slow. (c) **Similarity not stored/displayed yet** (see open questions). (d) **The Gemini path was not exercised live** (no API key, external calls off by default per AGENTS) — wiring is tested with an injected extractor returning Gemini-shaped candidates; no network call happened in any test. (e) **RULE-OCR-01's other legs are out of scope**: the confidence-threshold leg (0.75 default, PRD §8 step 2's vision-model fallback) and page-coverage/parser-error legs are not implemented — the vision fallback is off by default per AGENTS and plan line 82 names only the "match on page" leg. (f) **The frontend does not display grounding yet** (plan 5.11 is the consumer) — no `npm run build` needed or run; the key is additive JSON the current UI ignores. (g) Grounding is computed **at upload only** — a later page-text change (new document version) does not re-ground older fields (same "per-upload" semantics as bbox in 5.2).
- Open questions for a human: should a *grounded* model value ever carry more weight than an ungrounded one (today: no — both stay `unverified` until an officer verifies); should `similarity` be persisted and shown next to the status; calibration of `token_similarity`/`min_match_ratio` against real scans (the config note says so too); whether OCR values should get RULE-OCR-01's confidence leg (threshold 0.75 configurable, vision fallback) once that fallback exists; whether non-Latin tokenisation needs a script-aware path.
- Next suggested task: **Phase 5.4** (plan line 83) — `three_value.py`: Extraction / Claim / Master values, edit reason, RULE-EDIT-01 (FR-41).

### IMPL-0039 — Phase 5.2: per-field provenance — bbox, engine + version, page (plan line 81)
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-27 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / (this commit; gates green at this SHA)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 5 **5.2** (plan line 81: *"Store per-field bbox, confidence, engine+version, page (extend extracted-field model + migration)"*); PRD **FR-48** (split-screen review with highlight box — the box is what the UI will overlay) and **FR-39** (Tesseract with confidence + boxes; model/engine is configurable provenance). AGENT-04.
- Goal: every extracted field records **where on the page** it was read and **which program read it**, as stored data the UI can trust for a highlight and a checker can weigh — with honest NULL whenever a reader cannot say (never a guessed box or a made-up engine name).
- What existed before: `ExtractedField` already had `source_page`, `source_span`, `method` and `confidence`, but **no geometry** (plan item 12 — reviewer page-level view → bbox overlay — was blocked on it) and **no engine identity** (`method` said `pdf_text`/`ocr` but not pypdf vs its version); `FieldOut` exposed neither; no migration since `0015_scrutiny_results`.
- What I did (tests first — `backend/tests/test_field_provenance.py`, **10 tests, all ran, none skipped**):
  - **Model + schema** — `ExtractedField` gains `bbox` (JSON nullable), `engine` (String(64) nullable), `engine_version` (String(64) nullable); `ExtractedFieldCandidate` gains the same three; `FieldOut` exposes them (all optional, so pre-existing clients keep working).
  - **Line geometry, text layer (pypdf)** — `_read_pdf` now passes a `visitor_text` collector to `extract_text`: every text-showing operation records its string, transformed origin (tm×cm) and scaled font size. `_group_pdf_lines` groups chunks whose baselines are within half a font size (one visual line can be many PDF operations), then `_pdf_box` converts PDF user space (y up) to **top-left normalised fractions** with the box spanning ascender→descender (0.75 em above / 0.25 em below the baseline). Text-layer **widths are estimated** (char count × 0.5 em, a Helvetica-ish average — pypdf reports positions, not glyph advances) and the code says so where it is written: a highlight aid, never a measurement.
  - **Line geometry, OCR (Tesseract)** — `_ocr_image` feeds `image_to_data` to `_group_ocr_lines`, which unions the pixel rectangles of words sharing Tesseract's own `(block, par, line)` id — **exact** geometry, normalised against the image actually OCR'd (after any upscaling, so coordinates line up).
  - **Locating a field's box** — `_locate(page.lines, value, *label_needles)`: alnum-insensitive match of the stored value first, then the parser's own label (`label 'lessee'` from `method_note`) — the fallback that gives an ISO date (`2026-04-12`) the box of the `Sworn on 12 April 2026` line it was read from. Needles shorter than 3 alnum chars are refused (an "id" needle would hit any line), and **no match ⇒ `bbox: None`** — an unplaceable box would highlight the wrong part of the page, which is worse than no highlight. `_unit_box` rounds *before* clamping so `x+width`/`y+height` can never exceed 1.
  - **Engine provenance** — `_Page` carries `engine`/`engine_version`; text-layer pages report `pypdf` + `pypdf.__version__` (`_pypdf_version`, lru_cached), OCR pages `tesseract` + `get_tesseract_version()` (`_tesseract_version`, cached, only called after OCR already succeeded); the mock reports `engine="mock"` with no box/version (**it never opened the file**); the lease parser reports `grounded_lease` (deterministic path) or `gemini` + the model name (API path) with no box (it cites quotes, not geometry); seeded rows keep NULL (no engine ran). `_add` attaches all three per field.
  - **Storage + API** — `documents.py` persists the three keys on upload; `FieldOut` returns them. Officer verification still rewrites only `verification_status` (provenance is immutable to reviewers — tested).
  - **Migration** — `migrations/versions/f7e6eed3fa19_extracted_field_provenance_bbox_engine_.py` (`down_revision 0015_scrutiny_results`): three **nullable** `add_column`s via `batch_alter_table`, additive only — no destructive change, no approval gate. Docstring explains what/why.
- Files changed: `backend/app/models/application.py` (+3 columns + docstring), `backend/app/adapters/ocr/base.py` (+candidate keys), `backend/app/adapters/ocr/real.py` (visitor collection, `_Line`, `_group_pdf_lines`, `_group_ocr_lines`, `_unit_box`, `_pdf_box`, `_image_box`, `_locate`, `_label_needles`, `_add`, engine/version helpers, `_Page` extension), `backend/app/adapters/ocr/mock.py`, `backend/app/services/grounded_lease_extractor.py` (5 candidate sites), `backend/app/api/documents.py` (persist), `backend/app/schemas/api.py` (`FieldOut`), `backend/migrations/versions/f7e6eed3fa19_*.py` (new), `backend/tests/test_field_provenance.py` (new, 10), `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (5.2 ticked), `CONTEXT.md`, `README.md`, this log.

- API / schema / config changes: **3 new optional keys** on every field object in `document.fields` (upload response, document list, verify response unchanged); **1 new migration**; no new routes → no permission matrix rows; no new config (geometry constants live as commented code constants, not policy — they are rendering facts, not regulatory values).
- Permission and audit effects: **none** — reading provenance rides the existing `DOCUMENT_READ` routes; verification behaviour unchanged (still the only mutation, still audited as `document.field_verified`). Provenance is display/trust data: no rule, transition or outcome reads it (DEC-0003 intact — nothing here decides anything).
- Decisions made (extensions of DEC-0003; no new DEC id): (1) **Unknown provenance is NULL, never a guess** — no box ⇒ no highlight; no engine ⇒ no name. (2) **The label fallback exists because normalised values are not on the page** (ISO dates) and the parser's label *is* — placement follows how the value was actually read. (3) **Text-layer widths are estimated and documented as such** (pypdf gives no glyph advances); OCR boxes are exact — the `level: "line"` + engine fields let a consumer tell which is which. (4) **Verification does not touch provenance** — a human confirming a value does not change where or how a machine read it.
- Verification commands and results:
  - `pytest tests/test_field_provenance.py -v` → **10 passed, 0 skipped** (model columns; pypdf version + normalised box; two lines ⇒ different y; ISO-date label fallback; honest `None` for unplaceable; mock admits mock; grounded names itself; API round-trip + verify immutability; seeded NULLs; **Tesseract OCR provenance ran for real**)
  - `make lint` → **failed once** with `I001` unsorted imports in the generated migration file (ruff `--fix` corrected it; **BUG-0019**), then → "All checks passed!"
  - `make test` → **704 passed** in 65s (up from 694; +10). **0 skipped.**
  - `SIH_DATABASE_URL=sqlite:////tmp/sih.db alembic upgrade head` → `0015_scrutiny_results -> f7e6eed3fa19`; `alembic check` → "No new upgrade operations detected."
- Environment note (verified before committing, correcting an earlier draft of this line): **Tesseract 5.5.3 is present on this machine** at `/opt/homebrew/bin/tesseract` — the symlink is dated **24 Oct 2026**, i.e. installed *before* this task, not by it. Every suite run in this session reported **0 skipped**, and `pytest tests/test_document_extraction.py -v -rs` → **10 passed, 0 skipped**, so the OCR tests ran for real (the new `requires_ocr` test here included — 3 OCR-marked tests exist now: 2 in `test_document_extraction.py` + 1 in `test_field_provenance.py`). BUG-0001 already carries an Update (2026-10-26) recording that this machine has Tesseract and the tests run; it stays `Workaround` for hosts without the binary. The stale claim was CONTEXT's "Tesseract is not installed here, so the 2 OCR tests skip" note (added in `604fd54`, 27 Oct — wrong for this machine, contradicts BUG-0001's own update); it is corrected in this task's CONTEXT edit, and BUG-0001 gains an Update line noting the third OCR test and the re-verification.
- Limitations: (a) **Text-layer bbox width is an estimate** (documented in `_Line`); y/height (baseline + font size) are exact — good enough to highlight the right line, not to measure text. (b) `_locate` places a field on the **first** matching line: a value repeated on several lines (e.g. the same name in header and body) gets the first occurrence. (c) Multi-column/rotated PDFs are not layout-aware — chunks group by baseline across the full page width, so two columns' lines at the same y merge into one box (acceptable for the single-column AICTE documents in scope; noted here). (d) The Gemini path sets `engine_version` to the model *name*, not a snapshot hash — the model list is config, and calling out to Gemini did not happen in any test (no key present; external calls off by default). (e) Seeded rows have no bbox — the demo UI will show no highlight until a real upload; that is honest, not a bug. (f) The frontend does not yet render the overlay (plan 5.11 `SplitReview.tsx`/`EvidenceHighlight.tsx` is the consumer) — so no `npm run build` was needed or run; the new keys are additive JSON the current UI ignores.

### IMPL-0038 — Phase 5.1: `doc_classifier.py` for the five ingest classes (FR-38)
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-27 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / (this commit; gates green at this SHA)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 5 **5.1** (plan line 80); PRD **FR-38** (line 222: *"Accept PDF/scan/DOCX; classify"*, APH cl. 2.3.4 as cited) and the §8 pipeline step 1 (line 133: *"Ingest PDF / scan / DOCX; classify (EoA letter = master record, Format-2 NOC, Format-3 Resolution, lease, affidavit, other Annexure-2 item)"*). AGENT-04 (document intelligence).
- Goal: classify an uploaded document into the PRD's five named classes as **configuration + plain phrase matching** — no model call, no decision — with honest uncertainty and a mismatch that is flagged, never corrected (essentials: AI/OCR never decides, approves, rejects or blocks).
- What existed before: the upload pipeline (`md/2_ARCHITECTURE.md` §6) listed a `classify` step but **no classifier existed anywhere**; `ExtractionResult` carried only `fields`/`warnings` (no page text to classify from); the recorded `doc_type` is whatever the client declares (regex-checked vocabulary, no cross-check); the UI already displays `extraction.warnings`, so that was the natural advisory channel.
- What I did (tests first — `backend/tests/test_doc_classifier.py`, **19 tests**):
  - **Config** — new `backend/rules/doc_classifier.json`: `classifier_id RULE-DOC-CLASSIFY`, `status: proposed_unverified`, `placeholder`, `advisory_only`; `source` cites FR-38 + the §8 line-133 sentence; `human_review` states classification never changes the recorded type and never rejects/blocks; `note` records that the **phrase lists are placeholders pending the official Format-2/3 templates (PRD §19 asks whether they exist)**; `matching` (case/word-boundary flags + `text_weight: 3` vs `file_name_weight: 1` — page text outranks a typed file name, both configurable); five classes (`eoa_letter`, `format_2_noc`, `format_3_resolution`, `lease`, `affidavit`), each with `prd_name`, `label`, `phrases`, a `declared_doc_types` mapping grounded in `annexure_2.json`/upload vocabulary (EoA's mapping is a labelled placeholder — no upload type defines one) and a note; `fallback` for the PRD's *"other Annexure-2 item"* → `unclassified`. `policy.py` gains `doc_classifier_policy()`.
  - **Service** — new `backend/app/services/doc_classifier.py`: `config()`, `classes()`, and `classify(page_texts, file_name, declared_doc_type)` — pure, computed on call, stores nothing. Whitespace-normalised, word-boundary, case-insensitive phrase matching; per-class hits carry phrases, signal sources (`text`/`file_name`) and a weighted score; the unique top score classifies, an equal tie answers `ambiguous` (both candidates listed, label `None` — the classifier refuses to break ties by list order), no hits answers `unclassified`, no text answers `no_text` (**a file name alone never produces a label** — its hint is reported inside the note/hits only); `agrees_with_declared` compares the prediction's `declared_doc_types` with the uploader's `doc_type` (`True`/`False`/`None` when either side is absent); `mismatch_note` says the record is unchanged; `needs_review` is true unless it is a `classified` + text-evidenced + agreeing answer; the `note` composes every applicable caveat (BUG-0018).
  - **Reading the words** — `ExtractionResult.page_texts: list[str]` (new field, default empty): the real reader (`adapters/ocr/real.py`) fills it from the pages it already read, `GroundedLeaseExtractor` from its raw page texts, and the **mock leaves it empty on purpose** (it never reads bytes) so classification honestly answers `no_text` instead of pretending.
  - **API wiring** — `backend/app/api/documents.py`: after extraction, the upload runs `classify(...)`; a `mismatch_note` is appended to `extraction.warnings` (which the EvidencePanel already renders — **no frontend change needed**), and the 201 body gains `"classification": {…}`. The stored `doc_type` is untouched and no upload status ever changes because of it.
- Files changed: `backend/rules/doc_classifier.json` (new), `backend/app/services/policy.py` (+loader), `backend/app/services/doc_classifier.py` (new), `backend/app/adapters/ocr/base.py` (+`page_texts`), `backend/app/adapters/ocr/real.py` (+populate), `backend/app/services/grounded_lease_extractor.py` (+populate), `backend/app/api/documents.py` (+classify + warning + response key), `backend/tests/test_doc_classifier.py` (new, 19), `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (5.1 ticked), `CONTEXT.md`, `README.md`, `docs/BUG_LOG.md` (BUG-0018), this log.
- Existing code reused: the `policy.py` loader pattern, `ExtractionResult` as the reader→caller carrier, the upload response's `extraction.warnings` as the already-rendered advisory channel, `pdf_factory.text_pdf` for genuine text-layer fixtures, the `set_ocr_extractor`/`reset_ocr_extractor` injection pattern for the no-text test.
- API / schema / config changes: **1 new rule file**; **1 new optional response key** on `POST /applications/{id}/documents` (`classification`) + a possible extra warning string; `ExtractionResult` gains a defaulted field (dataclass, no migration). **No model change → no migration**; no new routes → no matrix rows; no frontend change (`UploadResult` simply doesn't declare the new key; warnings render as before).
- Permission and audit effects: none — classification rides the existing `DOCUMENT_UPLOAD` route and its `document.uploaded` audit; no new audit action (classification is not a state change), no permission changes.
- Decisions made (extensions of DEC-0003; no new DEC id): (1) **Classification is advisory output, never a mutation** — a mismatched declaration produces a warning on a 201, the record keeps the uploader's `doc_type`, and nothing blocks (PRD line 137: *"None blocks or rejects"*). (2) **Honest statuses over forced labels** — `no_text` / `unclassified` / `ambiguous` / filename-only all carry `needs_review` and no confident `label_id`; a classifier that always answers would be inventing. (3) **Ties refuse to decide** (equal weighted score → `ambiguous` with both candidates) rather than resolving by config order, which would be a hidden preference. (4) **Page text outranks the file name** (3 vs 1, both configurable) and filename-only evidence can never reach `needs_review: false`. (5) **Phrase lists are labelled placeholders** — the official Format-2/3 templates are an open §19 question; the lists are ordinary document names from the PRD sentence, not clause numbers, fees or deadlines, and the config says they are evidence, not authority. (6) **The class list is pinned to the PRD sentence by a test** that parses `md/1_PRD.md` line 133 — spec and config cannot drift. (7) **The mock answers `no_text`** — a reader that does not read bytes must not be reported as having classified anything.
- Verification commands and results:
  - `pytest tests/test_doc_classifier.py -q` → **19 passed** (after BUG-0018)
  - `ruff check app tests` → clean
  - `make lint` → "All checks passed!"
  - `make test` → **694 passed** in 62.2s (up from 675; +19). No skips reported.
- Bugs hit (logged when it happened): **BUG-0018** (1 failure — the note dropped the "file name only" caveat whenever a declaration mismatch also applied; both caveats are independent, so the note now composes them; fields were already correct).
- Limitations: (a) Classification runs **at upload only** — stored documents uploaded before this change have no classification on record; a read-side endpoint would need to re-read the stored file (candidate for a later task). (b) The **phrase lists are placeholder heuristics** — real Format-2/3 template wording is unknown (§19), so expect false positives/negatives on unfamiliar documents; every answer except a clean text match carries `needs_review` for that reason. (c) The **mock/demo reader classifies nothing** (`no_text`) — honest, but the demo shows real classification only with the real reader (the default). (d) The frontend does not yet *display* the `classification` block itself (only the mismatch warning via existing warnings) — plan 5.11's UI work is the place for that. (e) `eoa_letter`'s declared-type mapping is a placeholder: no upload vocabulary defines an EoA-letter document type. (f) DOCX (FR-38's "DOCX") is not readable by the current upload validation — unchanged by this task and still open.
- Open questions for a human: the official **Format-2 / Format-3 templates** (phrase-list authority, PRD §19); whether an EoA-letter upload type should exist in the vocabulary; whether DOCX acceptance is in scope for a later task; whether classification should be **stored** per document version (model change + migration) rather than computed at upload.
- Next suggested task: **Phase 5.2** (plan line 81) — per-field bbox/confidence/engine+version storage (extend extracted-field model + migration).

### IMPL-0037 — Phase 4 exit check: the full deficiency → compliance → Re-Scrutiny loop (plan line 77)
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-27 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / (this commit; gates green at this SHA)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 4 **exit check** (plan line 77: *"full deficiency → compliance → Re-Scrutiny loop for one seeded case"*); PRD **FR-06** (pre-check), **FR-14** (recorded results untouched), **FR-15** (deficiency report + compliance window + Re-Scrutiny handover). AGENT-06.
- Goal: prove the Phase 4 pieces (4.2 checklist, 4.3 results, 4.4 report/window, 4.5 RULE-DOC-01 staging, the existing deficiency loop) **compose** on one seeded case, end to end through the public API, with the audit trail carrying every step — without touching any product code (a test-only task unless the composition is broken).
- What existed before: `test_deficiency_loop.py` covered the officer-created deficiency loop (create → return → upload → addressed → resubmit → close) but bypassed 4.4's report and 4.5's pre-check entirely; nothing exercised issue → received → awaiting_re_scrutiny → close → Re-Scrutiny as one narrative.
- What I did (test first/only — `backend/tests/test_phase4_exit_check.py`, **2 tests**):
  - **The loop** (`test_the_full_deficiency_compliance_rescrutiny_loop`), 8 phases on `app-main-0001` (the only seeded route with return/resubmit legs — BUG-0014 keeps `existing_technical` out): (1) officer reads the RULE-DOC-01 pre-check (`one_or_more_required_documents_missing`, `needs_review`, exactly `affidavit_2` as candidate) and stages it — second POST idempotent, exactly 1 `Deficiency` row, case still `scrutiny`; (2) institution reads the report = `not_issued`, zero rows, `counts.draft == 0` (drafts never leak); (3) officer returns with `RC-DOC-MISSING` → draft promoted to `open`, report `issued`, `window_status: open`, `window_days > 0` (the §19 Q1 placeholder), institution now sees exactly the staged row; (4) institution uploads the corrective affidavit (201) — the test **observes** (not assumes) that the staged row does *not* auto-address: the `documents.py` matcher skips NULL `document_id` rows and matches `category == doc_type or doc_type in rule_key`, while a RULE-DOC-01 row is `document_id=None` + `category="document_deficiency"` — officer's explicit close is the designed path for pre-check rows; (5) the audited **demo clock advances one day** through the real Phase 3.8 `demo_clock.advance(db, admin, 1.0)` (BUG-0016), institution resubmits → report `received`, `resubmitted_at` set, outstanding > 0, `awaiting_re_scrutiny` true; (6) officer closes the row → `awaiting_re_scrutiny` false, counts `{draft 0, open 0, addressed 0, closed 1}`; (7) officer `start_scrutiny` → back to `scrutiny` (the Re-Scrutiny leg); (8) the audit trail, read in **`seq` order** (BUG-0017), carries `deficiency.doc_check_staged → deficiency.finalized → workflow.return_for_clarification → workflow.resubmit → workflow.start_scrutiny` in that chronology, plus exactly one accepted `deficiency.closed` on the row.
  - **The read-only property** (`test_the_report_and_pre_check_stay_read_only_through_the_loop`): a `Deficiency`/`AuditEvent` row-count snapshot around repeated `doc-check` and `deficiency-report` GETs is unchanged — the advisory reads store nothing (DEC-0003).
  - Clock isolation: an autouse fixture resets `set_clock(FixedClock(DEMO_NOW))` before and after each test so the one-day jump cannot leak (Phase 3 exit-check pattern).
- Files changed: `backend/tests/test_phase4_exit_check.py` (new, 2 tests), `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (line 77 ticked), `CONTEXT.md`, `README.md`, `docs/BUG_LOG.md` (BUG-0016, BUG-0017), this log. **No product code changed** — the composition held as designed.
- Existing code reused: `tests/conftest.py` fixtures (`client`, `as_user`, `access_for`, `database` per-test reseed, autouse `clock`), `pdf_factory.affidavit_pdf`, `demo_clock.advance` (Phase 3.8, audited), `AuditEvent.seq` for chronology.
- API / schema / config changes: **none** — no routes, no models, no migration, no config, no frontend. `alembic check` not needed; `npm run build` not needed.
- Permission and audit effects: none new — the test walks the already-pinned matrix routes (doc-check READ_ANY, staging/close TRANSITION_OR_VERIFY, transitions, upload, report).
- Decisions made (no new DEC id; extensions of DEC-0003): (1) **The strict `>` in `_resubmitted_at` is correct and stays** — a reply must come *after* the issue instant, and a same-instant resubmit from an earlier return cycle must not count as the reply to the latest one (ids are uuid4, timestamps are the only order); the exit check therefore moves the demo clock one day between return and resubmit instead of weakening the check. (2) **Audit chronology is read by `seq`** — never by natural result order. (3) **The upload→addressed gap for RULE-DOC-01 rows is documented, not "fixed"**: auto-addressing a pre-check row would require matching `affidavit_2 ↔ affidavit` (the labelled placeholder mapping) plus NULL-document semantics — a behaviour change needing its own task and probably human input on the mapping; the officer's explicit close (audited, tested here) is the correct path meanwhile.
- Verification commands and results:
  - `pytest tests/test_phase4_exit_check.py -q` → **2 passed** (after BUG-0016 and BUG-0017 were fixed)
  - `make lint` → "All checks passed!"
  - `make test` → **675 passed** in 60.2s (up from 673; +2). No skips reported.
- Bugs hit (logged when they happened): **BUG-0016** (report read `open` not `received` — same-instant return+resubmit under the fixed clock vs `_resubmitted_at`'s strict `>`; fixed in the test by advancing the audited demo clock, service correct) and **BUG-0017** (audit-order helper read rows in `entity_id` index order without `ORDER BY`, faking chronology; fixed by ordering on `AuditEvent.seq`).
- Limitations: (a) the loop runs on `new_technical` only — `existing_technical` still has no return/resubmit legs (**BUG-0014 Open**, route-table change needs human approval). (b) The composition observation about upload→addressed is **verified current behaviour**, recorded above; a pre-check row is closed manually by an officer. (c) A demo walkthrough that returns and resubmits *without* advancing the demo clock will read `open` rather than `received` — advance the clock (admin demo-clock) between the steps. (d) The test does not re-verify 4.3 per-item results inside the loop (they do not interact with the deficiency/window state; covered by their own suite).
- Open questions for a human: **BUG-0014** route gap; whether an upload *should* auto-address RULE-DOC-01 rows (needs the official Affidavit 1/2/3 mapping); §19 Q1 window days (the test only asserts `window_days > 0`).
- Next suggested task: **Phase 5.1** (plan line 80) — `doc_classifier.py` for the EoA letter, Format-2 NOC, Format-3 Resolution, lease and affidavit.

### IMPL-0036 — Phase 4.5: the RULE-DOC-01 document pre-check (FR-06)
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-27 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / (this commit; gates green at this SHA)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 4 **4.5** (plan line 76); PRD **FR-06** (line 183: *"Confirm Affidavit 2 and DSC-signed docs; list missing items as deficiency draft"*, APH cl. 2.3.4 as the PRD cites it) and the **RULE-DOC-01** row (line 145, outcome *"Deficiency candidate"*).
- Goal: evaluate the PRD's document-presence rule as configuration + pure lookups — three legs (required documents / Affidavit 2 / DSC signature) — and let an officer stage what is missing as **draft** deficiencies, without ever inventing a clause, never letting an unverified leg produce a pass, and never gating or moving anything (DEC-0003).
- What existed before: `md/rules.json` spelled RULE-DOC-01 out (`compliance_rules`, status `proposed_unverified`, four-value outcome vocabulary, `human_review: Required before issuing a formal deficiency`) but nothing evaluated it; the Phase 4.2 checklist (`checklist.py`) already joined the Annexure-2 list against uploads; `api/deficiencies.py` already created draft rows by hand; **no DSC or signature concept existed anywhere in the codebase** (grep over `backend/` found zero matches — DSC verification is a stub per `md/2_ARCHITECTURE.md` line 117), and the upload vocabulary has one generic `affidavit` type (Affidavit 1/2/3 cannot be told apart).
- What I did (tests first — `backend/tests/test_doc_check.py`, **24 tests**):
  - **Config** — new `backend/rules/doc_check_policy.json`: `rule_id RULE-DOC-01`, `status: proposed_unverified`, `placeholder: true`, `advisory_only: true`; `outcomes` copied **verbatim** from `md/rules.json` (and pinned to it by a test that reads the spec file, so the two can never drift); `outcome_precedence` (missing > checklist_unknown > all_present) with a note that the *order* is prototype policy since the spec defines the values but not their order; `human_review` from the spec; `inventory.source = annexure_2` (the 4.2 checklist); `affidavit_2.doc_types = ["affidavit"]` with a **PLACEHOLDER MAPPING** note; `dsc.checkable: false`, `status_when_unavailable: needs_review`, stub citation; and an `inventory_unavailable_note` saying `document_inventory_unavailable` (`PROCESSING FAILED`) is kept in the vocabulary but is unreachable with the local documents table as source. `policy.py` gains the `doc_check_policy()` loader.
  - **Service** — new `backend/app/services/doc_check.py`. `evaluate(db, application)` is **computed on read, never stored**: (1) *required-documents leg* = `checklist.case_checklist` — an untranscribed/unknown list answers `needs_review` with the checklist's own note, never an empty all-clear, and each `missing` line becomes a candidate keyed `annexure:{category_id}:{item_id}` (only non-rejected documents satisfy a line, mirroring the checklist rule); (2) *Affidavit 2 leg* — absent ⇒ `missing` + a candidate keyed `affidavit_2` (FR-06 certainly not met); present ⇒ `satisfied` but `confirmed: false` + needs-review, because the numbering cannot be distinguished; (3) *DSC leg* — **always needs_review**, `checkable: false`, never passed and never failed. Outcome chosen by the configured precedence; `needs_review` is a bool riding the whole response (true even on `PRE-CHECK PASSED`, where it means "presence only"); each candidate carries `existing_deficiency` when a live row already covers its line. `stage_drafts(db, application, actor_id)` turns **uncovered** candidates into `Deficiency(category="document_deficiency", rule_key="RULE-DOC-01", field_name=<line key>, status="draft")` — idempotent by line key; a `closed` row does **not** cover a line (a past acceptance must not hide a document gone missing since); returns `{created, skipped, outcome, outcome_label, needs_review}`.
  - **API** — `backend/app/api/workflow.py`: `GET /applications/{id}/doc-check` (`require_any(CASE_READ, APPLICATION_READ)`, scope-filtered, read-only — stores nothing) and `POST /applications/{id}/doc-check/drafts` (`require_any(CASE_TRANSITION, DOCUMENT_VERIFY)` — institution representatives hold neither, so staging is officer-only) which writes an audit event `deficiency.doc_check_staged` (actor, created/skipped ids, outcome) then commits. 2 new authz-matrix rows (READ_ANY; TRANSITION_OR_VERIFY).
  - **Frontend** — `types.ts`: `DocCheck`, `DocCheckLeg`, `DocCheckCandidate`, `DocCheckStaged`; `api.ts`: `api.docCheck(id)` + `api.stageDocCheckDrafts(id)`; `labels.ts`: `plainDocCheckOutcome`/`docCheckOutcomeTone`/`plainDocCheckLeg`/`docCheckLegStatus` (the server's own `outcome_label` is shown when present); `EvidencePanel.tsx`: a "Document pre-check (RULE-DOC-01)" panel **before** the deficiency card — outcome pill + "Needs review" pill, the three legs with status pills and their notes, the missing-item candidates (each flagged "Already drafted" when a live row exists), the `human_review` sentence, and a "Stage as draft deficiencies" button gated `canVerify || canRecordResult` whose tooltip says drafts stay officer-only.
- Files changed: `backend/rules/doc_check_policy.json` (new), `backend/app/services/policy.py` (+loader), `backend/app/services/doc_check.py` (new), `backend/app/api/workflow.py` (+2 endpoints, +imports), `backend/tests/test_authz_matrix.py` (+2 rows), `backend/tests/test_doc_check.py` (new, 24), `frontend/src/types.ts`, `frontend/src/api.ts`, `frontend/src/labels.ts`, `frontend/src/components/EvidencePanel.tsx`, `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (4.5 ticked), `CONTEXT.md`, `README.md`, `docs/BUG_LOG.md` (BUG-0015), this log.
- Existing code reused: `checklist.case_checklist` (the whole Annexure-2 inventory leg), the draft `Deficiency` row pattern from `api/deficiencies.py`, `require_any`/`require_application` scope filtering, `AuditService.append`, the 4.2 `Document.status != "rejected"` presence rule, the best-effort panel-loading pattern (a failed pre-check read never hides the documents).
- API / schema / config changes: **2 new routes** (1 read-only, 1 audited POST); **1 new rule file**; **no model change → no migration** (nothing to `alembic check`). One new accepted audit action (`deficiency.doc_check_staged`); no new permission — it reuses `case.read`/`application.read` for the read and `case.transition`/`document.verify` for staging, all pinned by the matrix.
- Permission and audit effects: the GET is a pure read (proved by a row-count/baseline snapshot test); the POST writes exactly one audit event per call (even when it creates nothing) and never moves the case; staged drafts are invisible to the institution in Phase 4.4's report (test asserts `not_issued` + zero rows after staging).
- Decisions made (extensions of DEC-0003; no new DEC id): (1) **The outcome vocabulary is `md/rules.json`'s own, pinned by a test** — if the config paraphrased a pass/fail label, the product would print wording the rule never authorised. (2) **The DSC leg can only be `needs_review`** — signature verification is a stub and no signature field exists, so "passed" would be an invention; the leg therefore makes `needs_review` true on every response, including on `PRE-CHECK PASSED`. (3) **Affidavit 2 → the single `affidavit` slot is a labelled placeholder** — present ≠ confirmed (`confirmed: false`), absent is a definite candidate; the numbered mapping is an open question for a human. (4) **Precedence (missing > unknown > present) is labelled prototype policy** — `md/rules.json` gives the values but not their order; a concrete missing document beats an unknown inventory because the deficiency is definite while the inventory gap is reported beside it as needs-review. (5) **Staging is a separate, audited officer act** — the GET only *names candidates*; drafts never issue, never reach the institution, never gate or move anything, and are idempotent per line so re-running the check or the POST changes nothing. (6) **A `closed` row does not cover its line** — history stays in the closed row and the audit trail; the present state (document missing again) must be raisable. (7) **`document_inventory_unavailable` stays in the vocabulary** (unreachable today, documented) rather than being silently dropped from the spec's four outcomes.
- Verification commands and results:
  - `pytest tests/test_doc_check.py -q` → **24 passed** (after BUG-0015 was fixed)
  - `pytest tests/test_authz_matrix.py -q` → **21 passed** with the 2 new rows
  - `make lint` → "All checks passed!"
  - `make test` → **673 passed** in 60.0s (up from 649; the +24 are the new suite). No skips reported.
  - `cd frontend && npm run build` → `✓ built in 726ms` (tsc + vite clean)
- Bugs hit (logged when it happened): **BUG-0015** (1 failure — my test asserted only the two Annexure-2 lines for a case that also lacks an affidavit; the service was right, the expectation was wrong; fixed in the test).
- Limitations: **(a)** Affidavit 2 is **not confirmable** here — one generic `affidavit` upload type, so "present" is satisfied-but-unconfirmed until an official Affidavit 1/2/3 mapping exists (open question below). (b) The **DSC leg is permanently `needs_review`** in this prototype (stub + no signature field): the pre-check can never confirm any document is DSC-signed, and no code path may ever turn that leg into a pass without real signature data. (c) The required-document inventory is only as good as the 4.2 Annexure-2 transcription — only t23-13 is transcribed (§19 Q8 open); on any other category the leg answers needs-review and only the affidavit candidate can be raised. (d) `document_inventory_unavailable`/`PROCESSING FAILED` is unreachable today (kept for spec fidelity). (e) The staging POST takes no payload — it always stages exactly what the current check names (no per-item selection UI yet). (f) The panel truncates long leg notes at 140 chars with the full text in a title tooltip.
- Open questions for a human: the **official Affidavit 1/2/3 numbering mapping** (which numbered affidavit FR-06 means and how to recognise it from an upload); **§19 Q8** Annexure-2 load order (blocks the inventory leg on 20 of 21 categories); whether a future signature source should flip `dsc.checkable` to true in config (that switch must be fed by real signature data, never assumed); **BUG-0014** still Open (the `existing_technical` route has no return/resubmit legs — unrelated to this task but still blocks the FR-15 loop on that route).
- Next suggested task: **Phase 4 exit check** (plan line 77) — full deficiency → compliance → Re-Scrutiny loop for one seeded case.

### IMPL-0035 — Phase 4.4: the deficiency report and the compliance window (FR-15)
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-27 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / (this commit; gates green at this SHA)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 4 **4.4** (plan line 75); PRD **FR-15** (line 187: *"Deficiency report, compliance window, compliance upload to Re-Scrutiny"*, APH cl. 2.3.3b/2.4 as the PRD cites it), with the reminder cadence from the §11 alerts table (line 281: *"Deficiency issued | Institution | On issue, 7d, 2d, due date | Regional on miss"*).
- Goal: give the institution **one artefact** — the deficiency report with its reply window — on top of the row loop that already existed, and give the sweep the PRD's reminder cadence for it, without inventing a window length, without storing anything, and without ever turning a deadline into a lock (DEC-0003).
- What existed before: the whole row-level loop (Phase 1–2): `api/deficiencies.py` creates **draft** rows (officer-only; the list endpoint already filters drafts from institutions), `return_for_clarification` **issues** them (promotes drafts → open, with an FR-28 reason code), the institution's corrective upload flips them to **addressed** (`documents.py`), the officer closes them, and `resubmit` (returned → submitted → `start_scrutiny`) is the Re-Scrutiny leg — all covered by `test_deficiency_loop`. What did **not** exist: a composed report read (issued-at, reply-by, open/overdue/received), a compliance *window* deadline with the §11 cadence, or any surfacing of the Re-Scrutiny handover. Nothing in the sweep knew about deficiencies.
- What I did (tests first — `backend/tests/test_compliance_report.py`, **25 tests**):
  - **Config** — new `backend/rules/compliance_policy.json`: `status: unverified`, `placeholder: true`, `advisory_only: true`, `window_days: 10` with `window_days_note` labelling it a demo value pending **PRD §19 Q1** (target days) or a recorded clause, and a `source` that cites FR-15 + the §11 line-281 cadence and states plainly that the window LENGTH appears nowhere in the PRD or `rules.json`/`rules2.json`. `backend/rules/deadline_policy.json` gains the `compliance` ladder — `compliance_7d` (offset −7, info), `compliance_2d` (offset −2, warning), `compliance_due` (offset 0, critical) — i.e. the PRD's own "7d, 2d, due date" as offsets from the window close, plus a note that the length is the placeholder. `policy.py` gains the `compliance_policy()` loader.
  - **Service** — new `backend/app/services/compliance.py`. `report_view(db, application, now, include_drafts=…)` is **computed on read**: the issue instant is the latest accepted `return_for_clarification` **transition row** (structured data, never free-text parsing); the close is `end_of_day(business_date(issue) + window_days − 1)` — the same end-of-day/calendar-day convention as the appeal and undertaking clocks. The answer carries `status` (`none`/`draft`/`not_issued`/`issued`), `issued_at`, `closes_at`, `window_status` (`not_started`/`open`/`overdue`/`received`), `days_remaining`, `overdue`, `resubmitted_at`, `awaiting_re_scrutiny`, per-status `counts`, the issued rows, a `note`, and the config's `placeholder`/`advisory_only`/`source`/`window_days` labels on every response. `compliance_window_item(db, application, now)` yields the sweep's `DeadlineItem` (alert type `compliance_window`, `cycle_key = compliance:{issue date}`) only while a report is issued and the institution has **not** resubmitted.
  - **Sweep** — `deadline_service.collect_items` section 6 iterates `current_state == "returned"` cases and appends the window item; `eligible_thresholds`'s kind map gains `"compliance_window" → "compliance"`. Alerts ride the existing idempotent dedupe path (`entity:alert_type:level:cycle_key`).
  - **API** — `backend/app/api/workflow.py`: `GET /applications/{id}/deficiency-report`, `require_any(CASE_READ, APPLICATION_READ)`, scope-filtered via `require_application`, read-only. The institution-detection mirrors `api/deficiencies.list_deficiencies` (institution-only ⇒ `include_drafts=False`). One new authz-matrix row (`READ_ANY`).
  - **Frontend** — `types.ts`: `DeficiencyReport` + `DeficiencyReportLine`; `api.ts`: `api.deficiencyReport(id)`; `labels.ts`: `plainReportStatus`/`reportStatusTone`/`plainWindowStatus`/`windowStatusTone` + a `compliance_window` alert label; `EvidencePanel.tsx`: the deficiency card now opens with a best-effort report summary — status pill, "Issued … · reply by …", window pill with days left, "Resubmitted …", an "Awaiting Re-Scrutiny" pill, and a "Placeholder window (10 days)" pill whose tooltip says it never blocks anything — plus the server's `note` (the overdue advisory sentence).
- Files changed: `backend/rules/compliance_policy.json` (new), `backend/rules/deadline_policy.json` (+ladder, +note), `backend/app/services/policy.py` (+loader), `backend/app/services/compliance.py` (new), `backend/app/services/deadline_service.py` (sweep section 6, kind map), `backend/app/api/workflow.py` (+1 endpoint, +import), `backend/tests/test_authz_matrix.py` (+1 row), `backend/tests/test_compliance_report.py` (new, 25), `frontend/src/types.ts`, `frontend/src/api.ts`, `frontend/src/labels.ts`, `frontend/src/components/EvidencePanel.tsx`, `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (4.4 ticked), `CONTEXT.md`, `README.md`, `docs/BUG_LOG.md` (BUG-0012/0013/0014), this log.
- Existing code reused: the draft→open→addressed→closed row loop and `return_for_clarification`'s draft promotion (Phase 1–2), `resubmit`/`start_scrutiny` route legs, `deadline_service.end_of_day` + `working_calendar.business_date` (the appeal-window maths pattern from 3.5), the sweep's `_get_or_create_alert` dedupe, `api/deficiencies.py`'s institution-draft filter as the visibility contract, `require_any`/`require_application` scope pattern.
- API / schema / config changes: **1 new read-only route**; **1 new rule file** + 1 ladder added to an existing one; **no model change, so no migration** (nothing to `alembic check`). No new permission and no new audit event — reading a report stores nothing (proved by a test comparing row counts + `baseline_version` + state before/after three reads).
- Permission and audit effects: read via `case.read`/`application.read` with the existing scope filter (other-institution case → 404, proven); institutions get `include_drafts=False`; no state change, no audit write, no gate touched. The window's expiry is raised only as alerts through the existing sweep (which already audits alert creation).
- Decisions made (all extensions of DEC-0003 + the "config, never hard-code" rule; no new DEC id): (1) **The issue instant is the accepted return transition** — structured data; the report never parses prose to guess when a notice went out. (2) **Before issue, the institution is *always* answered `not_issued`** — answering `none` when no drafts exist but `not_issued` when drafts do would leak the draft's existence through the status value alone. (3) **Window length is a labelled placeholder** (§19 Q1 open), the *cadence* is the PRD's own §11 wording; both labels ride on every response. (4) **Overdue never blocks** — the report says so in its `note`, and `test_an_expired_window_never_blocks_the_resubmission` proves a late institution is still let in. (5) **"Reply received" = resubmission**, not merely uploading corrections: the PRD's leg is "compliance upload **to Re-Scrutiny**", and only `resubmit` re-enters scrutiny; uploads alone are progress (`addressed` rows) but the case has not moved. Reminders stop on resubmit ("alerts stop when task closes"). (6) **`cycle_key` encodes the issue date**, so a re-return after Re-Scrutiny starts a fresh reminder cycle instead of reusing the old dedupe key. (7) **The report is computed on read, never stored** — same principle as the checklist and every clock here.
- Verification commands and results:
  - `pytest tests/test_compliance_report.py -q` → **25 passed** (after BUG-0012/0013 were fixed)
  - `pytest tests/test_authz_matrix.py tests/test_deficiency_loop.py tests/test_appeal_clock.py -q` → **45 passed** (the new matrix row agrees with the app)
  - `make lint` → "All checks passed!"
  - `make test` → **649 passed** in 59.1s (up from 624; the +25 are the new suite). No skips reported.
  - `cd frontend && npm run build` → `✓ built in 726ms` (tsc + vite clean)
- Bugs hit (logged when they happened): **BUG-0012** (11 failures — the seeded route I first drove has no return leg, and the return needs its FR-28 reason code; both fixed in the test), **BUG-0013** (3 failures — `select().count()` misuse, 23:59 asserted in UTC instead of the business timezone, re-return from `submitted` needing `start_scrutiny`; fixed in the test), **BUG-0014** (**Open** — see limitations).
- Limitations: **(a)** the FR-15 loop cannot actually be driven on `existing_technical` cases at all — that route defines no `return_for_clarification`/`resubmit` transitions (BUG-0014, Open; changing the route table is a human-approval gate, so the tests use `app-main-0001`/`new_technical` and nothing in this feature assumes a route). (b) The **window length is 10 days as a demo placeholder** — not an AICTE rule; do not read it as one. (c) The PRD's "on issue" alert leg is realised as *the report becoming visible in-app the moment the case is returned* plus sweep-driven 7d/2d/due reminders — this prototype's notifier is the sweep, not a real-time email/SMS dispatch (non-mock notifiers stay off per AGENTS.md). (d) The window counts **calendar** days (the §11 cadence is written in plain days and no compliance working-day rule exists in the PRD); the other clocks that must follow the working calendar can be switched in config if AICTE says otherwise. (e) The report's rows carry ids/categories/status but no deep link to the offending document page — the card below already lists documents. (f) No rate/volume work: the report re-queries deficiencies per read (fine at demo scale).
- Open questions for a human: **§19 Q1** — what the reply-window length actually is (replace `window_days` when answered); **BUG-0014** — should `existing_technical` gain the deficiency-return/resubmit transitions (my recommendation: yes, same two entries as `new_technical`, unless APH handles existing-technical applications differently — but that is a regulatory interpretation call); whether "Regional on miss" should map to the escalation chain the way stage-ageing does (today the ladder simply reaches `critical` severity at the due date).
- Next suggested task: **Phase 4.5** (plan line 76) — Affidavit 2 / DSC check, `RULE-DOC-01` (FR-06).

### IMPL-0034 — Phase 4.3: scrutiny result per checklist item and overall, with remarks
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-27 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / (this commit; gates green at this SHA)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 4 **4.3** (plan line 74); PRD **FR-14** (line 186: *"Result per checklist item and overall (Nil Deficiency / Deficiency) with remarks"*, APH cl. 2.4 as the PRD cites it), composing with **FR-13**'s checklist (4.2) and **FR-10**'s AA gate (1.7).
