- Goal: record the scrutiny officer's own verdict — per Annexure-2 checklist line and overall, both with remarks — using FR-14's own vocabulary, without the record ever contradicting itself, ever being attached to a line that does not exist, or ever moving/gating a case (DEC-0003).
- What existed before: the advisory checklist (4.2, IMPL-0033) generated the lines but nothing could record a verdict on them; `aa_stage_is_due(scrutiny_result, deficiencies_remaining)` (1.7) already defined the `nil_deficiency`/`deficiency` vocabulary via `aa_gate.scrutiny_results` but no endpoint or table ever produced a real scrutiny result for it to read — the gate had only ever been fed literals in tests. `Deficiency` rows tracked officer-raised issues separately.
- What I did (tests first):
  - **Models** — `backend/app/models/application.py`: `ScrutinyItemResult` (unique per application+category+item via `uq_scrutiny_item_result_line`; re-recording updates the row, audit keeps both judgements — same contract as `ReadinessFlag`) and `ScrutinyOverallResult` (one per application via `uq_scrutiny_overall_result_case`). Both FK-cascade on the application; `recorded_by` is a strict FK to `users.id` (BUG-0010 proved it stays strict).
  - **Migration** — `backend/migrations/versions/0015_scrutiny_results.py` (down_revision `0014_conflict_of_interest`): both tables + `ix_scrutiny_item_results_application_id` / `ix_scrutiny_overall_results_application_id`. First `alembic check` was red because the model said `unique=True` on the column while the migration created a named constraint; fixed by aligning both sides on the repo's named-constraint style (BUG-0011). Verified `upgrade head` then `check` → "No new upgrade operations detected."
  - **Service** — new `backend/app/services/scrutiny_results.py`. `result_vocabulary()` reads `routing.aa_gate()["scrutiny_results"]` — the PRD's own FR-14 wording already carried by the FR-10 gate; nothing invents a third value. `record_item_result()` refuses (`ValueError`) an unknown result value, a category the case does not record (no `ApplicationItem` row → no line → "needs review"), an item outside the category's configured list, and an **untranscribed list** (§19 Q8 — missing data, never accepted as an empty list). `record_overall_result()` refuses the **self-contradiction**: overall `nil_deficiency` while any item result is `deficiency` (Nil means no deficiency anywhere); the reverse is allowed because a deficiency may exist outside the checklist (compliance findings, document contents). `results_view()` joins the 4.2 checklist with the recorded rows on read, surfaces an `orphaned_item_results` list for results whose config line disappeared (**kept and flagged, never dropped** — a rule-file edit must not erase an officer's judgement), counts (`items_configured`/`items_recorded`/`items_deficiency`/`open_deficiencies`), `derived_overall` (None until every line is decided — never guessed), the composed FR-10 `aa_gate` answer (`open`/`closed`/`needs_review`), and a joined `note` explaining any needs-review condition.
  - **API** — `backend/app/api/workflow.py`: `GET /applications/{id}/scrutiny-results` (`require_any(CASE_READ, APPLICATION_READ)`, scope-filtered, read-only), `POST /applications/{id}/scrutiny-results` and `POST .../scrutiny-results/overall` (both `require(CASE_TRANSITION)` — a verdict is an officer act, never the applicant's; `KeyError`/`ValueError` → 422 with the service's own words; audit events `scrutiny.item_result_recorded` / `scrutiny.overall_result_recorded` with before/after and the result in `reason`; single commit after the audit append). Three matrix rows + two JSON probe bodies added to `tests/test_authz_matrix.py`.
  - **Frontend** — `types.ts`: `ScrutinyItemResultOut`/`ScrutinyOverallResultOut`/`ScrutinyResultsView`; `api.ts`: `api.scrutinyResults(id)` + `recordScrutinyItemResult`/`recordScrutinyOverallResult`; `labels.ts`: `plainScrutinyResult` (Nil Deficiency / Deficiency / "Not decided yet"), `scrutinyResultTone`, `plainAaGate`; `EvidencePanel.tsx`: loads the results view best-effort beside the checklist, and — gated on a new `canRecordResult` prop (`case.transition`, passed from `CaseView`) — renders per-line remarks input + Nil Deficiency / Deficiency buttons and an overall select + remarks + Record button, showing the recorded verdict, the derived-overall hint, and the FR-10 gate answer as advisory pills. A 422 (contradiction, unknown line) is shown as the server's own message, never swallowed. `npm run build` ✓.
  - **Backend tests** — new `backend/tests/test_scrutiny_results.py` (**24 tests**): the vocabulary is the gate's, not ours; unknown value refused; item result recorded with remarks; re-record updates the single row; unrecorded category refused; item outside the list refused; untranscribed list refused as needs-review; overall recorded with remarks; overall `nil_deficiency` refused while an item is deficient **and not stored**; overall `deficiency` with all items clean allowed; overall `nil_deficiency` with all-clean items allowed; re-record keeps one row; view joins + counts + derived; derived stays None until every line decided; `aa_gate` composition needs_review→closed→open; orphaned result kept + flagged with the exact dict; no-category case answers needs_review with a note; recording never moves the case (state/version/transitions unchanged); API happy path via `scrutiny@demo.local`; API refuses the contradictory overall with 422; audits written; 401 without token; institution 403; scope-filtered 404 on `app-second-0002`.
- Files changed: `backend/app/models/application.py` (+2 models), `backend/app/models/__init__.py` (exports), `backend/migrations/versions/0015_scrutiny_results.py` (new), `backend/app/services/scrutiny_results.py` (new), `backend/app/api/workflow.py` (+2 request models + 3 endpoints), `backend/tests/test_authz_matrix.py` (3 rows + 2 probe bodies), `backend/tests/test_scrutiny_results.py` (new, 24), `frontend/src/types.ts` (+3 interfaces), `frontend/src/api.ts` (+3 methods), `frontend/src/labels.ts` (+3 helpers), `frontend/src/components/EvidencePanel.tsx` (+results section + 2 handlers + `canRecordResult`), `frontend/src/components/CaseView.tsx` (passes `canRecordResult`), `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (4.3 ticked), `CONTEXT.md`, `README.md`, `docs/BUG_LOG.md` (BUG-0010, BUG-0011), this log.
- Existing code reused: `routing.aa_gate()`/`aa_stage_is_due()` (1.7 — the vocabulary and gate the results now feed for real), `checklist.case_checklist()`/`category_items()` (4.2 — the lines being judged), `AuditService.append` pattern, `require`/`require_any` + scope-filter pattern from the sibling workflow routes, `ReadinessFlag`'s re-record-updates-row contract, `EvidencePanel`'s best-effort parallel load.
- API / schema / config changes: **2 new tables** (migration 0015); **3 new routes** (1 read, 2 write). No new permission (`CASE_READ`/`APPLICATION_READ`/`CASE_TRANSITION` already exist) and no new rule file — the vocabulary stays in `workflow_routes.json`'s `aa_gate.scrutiny_results` where FR-10 put it.
- Permission and audit effects: recording requires `case.transition` (scrutiny/regional/administrator; institution 403, proven); every recording writes an audit event; refused recordings raise 422 before any row is written; recording never transitions a case (proven by a test asserting state/baseline_version/transition-count unchanged).
- Decisions made: (1) **Two tables, not one** — an overall verdict and per-line verdicts have different cardinality and different consistency rules; forcing one table would need nullable columns and ambiguous reads. (2) **Self-consistency, one direction only** — `nil_deficiency` overall is refused while any item is `deficiency`; `deficiency` overall with clean items is fine (a deficiency may live outside the Annexure-2 checklist — compliance findings, document contents). The reverse refusal would force officers to lie about real deficiencies. (3) **Vocabulary never duplicated** — the service reads `aa_gate.scrutiny_results`; adding a third value would be a config change, reviewed as one. (4) **A result needs a real line** — category must be recorded on the case (`ApplicationItem`) *and* the item must be in the configured list; an untranscribed list is refused as needs-review rather than treated as empty (same honesty rule as 4.2). (5) **Orphans kept** — a recorded verdict whose config line disappears is surfaced in `orphaned_item_results` with a note, never deleted: an officer's judgement must not evaporate because a rule file changed. (6) **`derived_overall` stays None until every line is decided** — a guessed overall is exactly the "silently pass missing data" failure the Master Prompt forbids. (7) No new DEC entry — this is DEC-0003's advisory pattern plus FR-14's own vocabulary; the workflow transition remains the separate audited act that moves a case.
- Verification commands and results:
  - `pytest tests/test_scrutiny_results.py tests/test_authz_matrix.py -q` → after fixing BUG-0010/BUG-0011: **24 passed** + matrix green (44 passed together)
  - `SIH_DATABASE_URL=sqlite:////tmp/sih_check_43.db alembic upgrade head && alembic check` → migration applies; **"No new upgrade operations detected."**
  - `make lint` → "All checks passed!" (after combining a nested `if` — SIM102 — and dropping an unused `checklist` import)
  - `make test` → **624 passed** in 56.6s (up from 600 at `801f9da`; the +24 are the new suite). No skips reported.
  - `cd frontend && npm run build` → `✓ built in 706ms` (tsc + vite clean)
- Limitations: recording does **not** link a verdict to the specific document/field that justified it (a `Deficiency` row can, and 4.4 will compose them) — the verdict is per checklist line plus free-text remarks. The UI's per-line controls appear only for lines the configured list knows (t23-13 today); a case with an untranscribed category shows the needs-review note and no controls, which is honest but means 20 categories cannot be judged until §19 Q8 is answered. `recorded_by` is shown as a raw user id in the view — a display name join is future work. The FR-10 gate composition uses open `Deficiency` rows (status open/addressed) as `deficiencies_remaining`; that matches the gate's contract but the number is not yet surfaced in the UI beyond the gate pill.
- Open questions for a human: §19 Q8 (which Annexure-2 lists to transcribe next — until then most cases cannot record per-line verdicts); whether the official APH cl. 2.4 vocabulary is exactly {Nil Deficiency, Deficiency} or carries more values (today the config can be extended without code, but the PRD's wording is what's recorded); whether an overall verdict should eventually *require* a linked reason code.
- Next suggested task: **Phase 4.4** (plan line 75) — deficiency draft → compliance window → upload → Re-Scrutiny (FR-15).

### IMPL-0033 — Phase 4.2: Annexure-2 checklist beside the uploaded documents
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-27 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / (this commit; gates green at this SHA)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 4 **4.2** (plan line 73); PRD **FR-13** (line 185: *"Generate checklist from category's Annexure-2 list beside uploaded docs"*) and **§19 Q8** (line 360: which APH sections / Annexure-2 lists load into the checklist engine first — open for AICTE).
- Goal: show, next to a case's uploaded documents, which documents the case's Table 2.3 category's Annexure-2 list names and which of them this case has uploaded — as advisory presentation only (DEC-0003), never a gate, and with the missing/uncertain data said out loud instead of papered over.
- What existed before: `Application.category` is a free-text route label (e.g. "existing_technical"); `ApplicationItem` rows exist for combined cases but nothing in production records Table 2.3 category ids on them, so no checklist source existed. There was no annexure config, no checklist service, no endpoint, no UI block. The PRD's Annexure-2 lists themselves are not transcribed anywhere in the repo except the recorded NOC research in `things.md` §1 (read from the APH 2024-27 PDF with printed page numbers), which covers only the Change of Site scenario.
- What I did (tests first):
  - **Rule file** — new `backend/rules/annexure_2.json`: one entry per each of the 21 Table 2.3 categories (`status: unverified`, `placeholder: true`, `advisory_only: true`, `source` citing FR-13 + §19 Q8 + `things.md` §1). Only **t23-13 (Change of Site / Location)** carries a transcribed item list — three items, each with its own printed-page source: `noc_format_2` (Format-2 university NOC → `doc_type university_noc`), `resolution_format_3` (Format-3 Trust/Society resolution → `format_3_resolution`), `new_site_proof` (lease deed or ownership record of the new site → `lease_deed`/`lease_agreement`/`ownership_record`). Every other category ships `items: []` plus an explicit `pending_note` ("list not yet transcribed — PRD §19 Q8 open. Needs review; an empty list here does NOT mean nothing is required"), so an empty list can never read as "nothing required".
  - **Service** — new `backend/app/services/checklist.py`. `category_items(category_id)` raises `KeyError` for an unknown category (fail loudly, never a default empty list); `case_checklist(db, application)` joins the configured list against the documents that exist right now and is **computed on read, never stored** — it cannot go stale. Three honesty rules: (1) a case with no recorded Table 2.3 category answers `needs_review` with an explanatory note (nothing in production creates `ApplicationItem` category rows yet — this is the normal state of a seeded case, said out loud); (2) a recorded category the routing table no longer recognises, or one whose list is not transcribed, surfaces as `needs_review` per category — never a 500 and never an empty all-clear; (3) **presence is not validity** — an uploaded (non-rejected, `status != "rejected"` — the same convention `portal_feed`/`reporting_service` use) document marks an item `uploaded`; whether it is genuine, signed or sufficient is scrutiny's job. Overall status is the blurriest category: any `needs_review` wins over `incomplete` wins over `complete`.
  - **Loader** — `policy.annexure_2_policy()` (lru_cached) in `backend/app/services/policy.py`, same pattern as every other rules file.
  - **API** — new `GET /applications/{application_id}/checklist` in `backend/app/api/workflow.py`, gated `require_any(CASE_READ, APPLICATION_READ)`, scope-filtered like the sibling reads (an other-institution case 404s). Returns the service dict verbatim: `status`, `placeholder`, `source`, `note`, `categories[]` (`category_id`, `category`, `status`, `note`, `uploaded`, `total`), `items[]` (`category_id`, `item_id`, `title`, `doc_types`, `status`, `source`, `note`). One matrix row added to `tests/test_authz_matrix.py`.
  - **Frontend** — `frontend/src/types.ts` gains `ChecklistItem`/`ChecklistCategory`/`ChecklistOut`; `frontend/src/api.ts` gains `api.checklist(id)`; `EvidencePanel.tsx` loads the checklist best-effort alongside documents (a checklist failure can never hide the files) and renders an advisory block beside the document list: an "Unverified list" pill when the config is a placeholder, per-category count + status pills, one line per item (Uploaded / Not uploaded), the pending note for untranscribed categories, the no-category note when nothing is recorded, and a closing source line. Plain-language copy ("A guide to what this kind of application usually needs… It is not a decision — a checker still reads every file"), no emoji.
  - **Backend tests** — new `backend/tests/test_annexure_checklist.py` (**18 tests**): config is labelled placeholder/advisory; all 21 categories have an entry; only t23-13 is transcribed and every other entry says "needs review"; the transcribed items cite their sources; an unknown category raises `KeyError`; a case without recorded categories answers `needs_review` with the explanatory note; a change-of-site case with nothing uploaded is `incomplete` (3 × missing) — run on `app-draft-0004`, the one seeded case with no documents, because `app-existing-0003` ships `lease_deed` + `ownership_record` which already satisfy the new-site-proof line; uploading the named documents flips exactly those lines to `uploaded`; a fully uploaded case reads `complete`; a rejected document does not satisfy an item; an untranscribed category reads `needs_review` with no items; a mixed case is only as clear as its blurriest category; an unrecognised recorded category needs review, not a crash; the computation writes nothing (no audit event, no state/version change); uploads appear on the next read without any refresh step; the endpoint serves a signed-in officer, demands a token (401) and is scope-filtered (404).
- Files changed: `backend/rules/annexure_2.json` (new), `backend/app/services/policy.py` (+`annexure_2_policy()`), `backend/app/services/checklist.py` (new), `backend/app/api/workflow.py` (+endpoint + import), `backend/tests/test_authz_matrix.py` (1 row), `backend/tests/test_annexure_checklist.py` (new, 18), `frontend/src/types.ts` (+3 interfaces), `frontend/src/api.ts` (+`checklist`), `frontend/src/components/EvidencePanel.tsx` (+checklist block, best-effort load), `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (4.2 ticked), `CONTEXT.md`, `README.md`, this log. **No migration** — nothing new is stored; the checklist is derived on read.
- Existing code reused: `policy.py`'s cached-loader pattern, `routing.table_2_3_rows()`/`category()` for the 21-category vocabulary, `require_any(CASE_READ, APPLICATION_READ)` + the scope-filter helper the sibling workflow reads use, the `Document.status != "rejected"` presence convention, `EvidencePanel`'s existing `load()`/best-effort-catch pattern (as deficiencies already used).
- API / schema / config changes: **1 new read-only route**; **1 new rule file** (labelled placeholder). No new permission — the route reuses `CASE_READ`/`APPLICATION_READ` the relevant roles already hold.
- Permission and audit effects: read-only; no audit event written (nothing changes state), proven by a test.
- Decisions made: (1) **Computed on read, never stored** — storing a checklist would let it go stale the moment a document is uploaded or replaced; the join is cheap and always current. (2) **Empty list ⇒ needs review, never "nothing required"** — a missing transcription is missing data; 20 of 21 categories are untranscribed (§19 Q8 open), and silently implying "no documents needed" would be a wrong regulatory signal. (3) **Only t23-13 transcribed** — it is the only scenario the recorded research covers (`things.md` §1); inventing lists for the other 20 categories would violate the "never invent APH clauses" rule. (4) **Presence is not validity** — the checklist marks a line present when a non-rejected document of a named type exists; it never judges genuineness/signature/sufficiency (scrutiny's job, and RULE-DOC-01/RULE-LEASE-01's). (5) **Per-category note rides the single `note` key** — every category dict (transcribed, pending, unrecognised) uses one `note` field, so the UI reads one key regardless of status. (6) No new DEC entry — this is the advisory policy-as-data pattern of DEC-0003 applied to FR-13; no new regulatory value, state or route.
- Verification commands and results:
  - `pytest tests/test_annexure_checklist.py -q` → **18 passed** (1.63s)
  - `make lint` → "All checks passed!" (after fixing an import-order I001 in the new test file)
  - `make test` → **600 passed** in 53.5s (up from 582 at `adebb96`; the +18 are the new suite). No skips reported.
  - `cd frontend && npm run build` → `✓ built in 726ms` (tsc + vite clean)
- Limitations: only the Change of Site list is transcribed; the other 20 categories answer "needs review" until a human records their Annexure-2 lists (§19 Q8). The checklist matches documents by `doc_type` only — it does not check contents, signatures or dates (by design). The upload form's `DOC_TYPES` list does not yet offer `university_noc`/`format_3_resolution` as choices, so on the demo UI those two lines can only be satisfied by an API upload or future UI work; the checklist itself is honest about their absence. No OCR/extraction link — the checklist does not claim a document *contains* what the line asks for.
- Open questions for a human: §19 Q8 — which Annexure-2 lists should be transcribed and verified next, and by whom; the transcribed t23-13 items remain `unverified` until someone records the exact Annexure-2 clause in `docs/SOURCES.md`.
- Next suggested task: **Phase 4.3** (plan line 74) — per-item and overall scrutiny result with remarks (FR-14).

### IMPL-0032 — Phase 3 exit check: demo clock → alerts at lead times → L0→L1→L2 → 0 drift
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-27 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / (this commit; gates green at this SHA)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 3 **exit check** (plan line 69); PRD **FR-09** (stage graph), **FR-27** (appeal clock — swept alongside), **FR-33** (deposit clock — swept alongside), **§11** (alerts/escalation ladder and the five-minute demo narrative, line 334: "a demo clock that advances time to show reminders and escalation").
- Goal: prove the plan's Phase 3 exit check end to end — *advance demo clock → alerts fire at configured lead times → escalate L0→L1→L2; reconciliation shows 0 diff* — as executable evidence on one seeded case, composing the individually-tested pieces (3.3 stage limits, 3.4 escalation, 3.8 demo clock, 3.9 reconcile) into the demo narrative.
- What existed before: every piece had its own suite — `test_alerts.py` (threshold ladders), `test_escalation.py` (level lookups + one sweep snapshot at a fixed instant), `test_demo_clock.py` (advance/reset mechanics), `test_reconcile.py` (0-drift after a blind 30-day jump). What did **not** exist was the composed proof: no test moved the demo clock *through the real `demo_clock.advance` service* in steps, swept at each stop, and asserted the alert set, the escalation level, the recipient widening and a clean reconciliation at every rung. Nothing was missing from production code — the exit check is a verification task, and the answer was green with no product change.
- What I did (tests first): new `backend/tests/test_phase3_exit_check.py` (**7 tests**), driving the seeded `app-existing-0003` (existing_technical / scrutiny, configured 10-day placeholder limit) with `current_state_since` pinned to the demo-clock baseline so each jump lands exactly on a configured threshold:
  1. *silence before the lead time* — day 0 of the stage, sweep fires nothing (the first lead time is day 8 = limit−2 per `deadline_policy.json`).
  2. *due_soon → L0* — after `demo_clock.advance(8)` (80% of the limit) the sweep creates exactly one `stage_ageing` alert whose payload carries `escalation_level: L0`, `sla_days: 10`, `days_in_state: 8`.
  3. *overdue → L1* — day 10 (100%) the `overdue` alert appears with `escalation_level: L1`, and the region's regional officer is among its recipients (severity critical widens the audience — the §11 "L1 at 100%" beat, resolved by email lookup, not a guessed id).
  4. *escalated → L2* — day 15 (150%) the `escalated` alert appears with `escalation_level: L2` and the supervisory officer (placeholder Regulation Bureau) among recipients.
  5. *one idempotent walkthrough* — a single test walks 0 → 8 → 10 → 15 days, asserting the exact alert set at each stop, `reconcile.reconcile(db).zero_differences` at each stop, and that a re-sweep on the same clock creates **0** new alerts (dedupe_key encodes the stage's cycle).
  6. *the escalation endpoint agrees* — after the jumps, `GET /applications/{id}/escalation` reports `days_in_stage: 15`, `percent_used: 150`, `level: L2` — the advisory snapshot and the sweep read one clock.
  7. *the API path drives the same ladder* — `POST /internal/deadline-sweep` (admin) after a jump produces the same alert, reports `created: 0` on the repeat, and `GET /internal/reconcile-clocks` answers `zero_differences: true` — the exact calls the demo makes.
  An autouse fixture resets the process-wide `FixedClock` to `DEMO_NOW` before and after every test, so no jump leaks into another suite (the clock is global state; BUG-0003's lesson).
- Files changed: `backend/tests/test_phase3_exit_check.py` (new, 7 tests), `docs/BUG_LOG.md` (BUG-0009), `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (Phase 3 exit check ticked), `CONTEXT.md`, this log. **No production code changed** — the exit check verified the composed behaviour as built.
- Existing code reused: `demo_clock.advance` (the real audited clock move, not a hand-rolled `set_clock`), `deadline_service.sweep`, `reconcile.reconcile`, the `GET .../escalation` and `/internal/*` endpoints, the `db`/`access_for`/`as_user` conftest fixtures.
- API / schema / config / migration changes: none.
- Permission and audit effects: none new — the test exercises the existing `ADMIN_USERS` gates on demo-clock and sweep, and every `demo_clock.advance`/sweep audit event is written by the existing services.
- Decisions made: (1) **Time moves through `demo_clock.advance`, not `set_clock`** — the exit check is about the demo *control*, so the test must use the same audited service the demo panel calls. (2) **Pin `current_state_since` rather than compute offsets from the seed** — the seeded 12-day age would have made the first jump land mid-ladder; pinning to the baseline makes each stop land exactly on a configured threshold (8/10/15 days for 80/100/150%). (3) No new DEC entry — this is verification of already-decided behaviour (DEC-0003 advisory escalation, labelled placeholder thresholds); it introduces no new regulatory value, state or route.
- Verification commands and results:
  - `pytest tests/test_phase3_exit_check.py -v` → **7 passed** (1.07s)
  - `make lint` → "All checks passed!" (after BUG-0009's fix)
  - `make test` → **582 passed** in 55.7s (up from 575 at the merged baseline 959f141; the +7 are the new suite). The 2 known Tesseract-absence OCR skips (BUG-0001) no longer appear on this machine's run — 582 is a clean pass with no skips reported.
- Limitations: the walk exercises one case on one route (existing_technical / scrutiny). The other routes and the non-stage-ageing ladders (undertaking, document-expiry, EVC-visit, appeal-window) each keep their own phase tests but are not re-proven in this composed walkthrough. The alert *delivery* is the mock notifier (log file) — "delivered" is asserted by the sweep counters in `test_alerts.py`, not by reading a real channel.
- Open questions for a human: none — the exit check passes as written in the plan.
- Next suggested task: **Phase 4.2** (plan line 73) — Annexure-2 checklist generated from the category list beside the documents (FR-13), starting with the Change of Site list.

### IMPL-0031 — Phase 4.1: assignment by workload + conflict-of-interest declaration and hiding
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-27 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / dfb42f2 (gates green at this SHA)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 4 **4.1** (AGENT-07 assignment/COI); PRD **FR-12** ("assignment by Region and workload; conflict-of-interest declaration and hiding") and the plan line-72 task. Also honours PRD **Appendix E, line 412**: *"Do not claim COI handling exists without approved policy and tests"* — there is no approved COI policy in the PRD, so this ships the honest minimum (record + hide) as clearly-labelled placeholder configuration, never a substantive conflict rule.
- Goal: put a case in the queue of the *least-loaded* eligible officer, and let an officer declare a conflict of interest on a case they can see — after which the case is hidden from them and they are kept out of the assignment pool for it — without letting a declaration ever gate, move or reject a case.
- What existed before: `case_assignment_service.assign_next_actors` picked candidates from the route table's `allowed_roles` plus jurisdiction, in arbitrary order, and knew nothing about conflicts. `authorization_service.scope_filter` / `can_access_application` decided visibility from role scope plus an active `CaseAssignment` row, and likewise knew nothing about conflicts. There was no `ConflictOfInterest` model, no declaration API, no `assignment_policy.json`, and no workload ordering. The PRD's Appendix E COI schema (declaration, type, timestamp, reviewer, exclusion, reassignment) was entirely unimplemented.
- What I did (tests first):
  - **Rule file** — new `backend/rules/assignment_policy.json`, a labelled placeholder (`status: unverified`, `placeholder: true`, `advisory_only: true`, `source` citing FR-12 / Appendix E). It declares the workload `strategy: "lowest_active_load"` and the COI `effect: "hide_and_exclude_from_pool"`. Loaded by the new cached `policy.assignment_policy()` so the ordering rule is configuration, not hard-coded — consistent with every other phase's labelled placeholder.
  - **Model + migration** — new `ConflictOfInterest` in `backend/app/models/identity.py` (id, application_id, declarant_id, conflict_type, description, declared_at, status, reviewer_id, reviewed_at, resolution_note; unique on `(application_id, declarant_id)`) plus `backend/migrations/versions/0014_conflict_of_interest.py`. `alembic upgrade head` then `alembic check` on a fresh DB → `No new upgrade operations detected`.
  - **Service** — new `backend/app/services/conflict_of_interest.py`. `declare(...)` records a declaration (one open per declarer per case — re-declaring returns the existing row, so a retry cannot create a second hold); `review(...)` sets it to `resolved`/`revoked`, which restores visibility and pool eligibility; `has_open_coi(...)` and `open_coi_case_ids(...)` are the two read hooks the assignment and visibility paths call. The `effect` is **hiding-only**: it removes the case from the declarer's view and pool and nothing else.
  - **Workload ordering + COI in assignment** — `assign_next_actors` now sorts candidates by fewest active (unrevoked) assignments when `strategy == "lowest_active_load"` and `continue`s past any candidate with an open declaration on this case. The soft cap is ordering only: an over-cap officer is ordered last, never dropped, so a queue can never be left empty by a placeholder formula. Jurisdiction is still enforced after the sort — ordering never grants a case to an officer whose scope does not cover it.
  - **COI hiding in visibility** — surgical, per-declarer. `scope_filter`'s `assigned`-scoped clause is now `assigned_clause AND NOT conflicted` (a `ConflictOfInterest` subquery on `status == "open"` for this user); `can_access_application` returns `False` for an assigned-scoped officer with an open declaration on that case. Region, institution and committee scope are untouched, so a regional officer who declared a COI still sees their region — hiding is per-declarer, not a case-wide lock.
  - **API** — new `backend/app/api/conflict_of_interest.py`: `POST/GET /applications/{id}/conflicts-of-interest` (`require_any(CASE_READ, MONITORING_READ)`) and `POST /conflicts-of-interest/{declaration_id}/review` (`require(MONITORING_READ)`). **Declaring deliberately does *not* re-check the declarer's own visibility**: a declaration hides the case from the declarer, so a naive visibility check would refuse the very declaration that caused it and break re-declaration. An out-of-scope or nonexistent id still looks absent (404), because such a caller was never assigned the case and holds no declaration on it. Every declare/review is audited via `AuditService` (`coi.declared` / `coi.reviewed`). Three matrix rows added to `tests/test_authz_matrix.py`; the in-scope probe now runs reads before writes, because a declare probe legitimately hides the case from the declarer.
  - **Backend tests** — new `tests/test_assignment_coi.py` (**12 tests**): workload ordering hands the case to the least-loaded officer first; workload ordering does not override jurisdiction; an open declaration hides the case from the declarer but *not* from another officer who also holds it (per-declarer); a declaration keeps the declarer out of the assignment pool while the conflict-free officer still gets the case (the non-empty-pool control is what makes the exclusion observable — an earlier draft of this test passed vacuously on an empty pool); re-declaring returns the same row; reviewing restores visibility; the declare and review endpoints enforce their permission gates (401/403).
- Files changed: `backend/rules/assignment_policy.json` (new), `backend/app/services/policy.py` (`assignment_policy()` loader), `backend/app/services/conflict_of_interest.py` (new), `backend/app/models/identity.py` (+`ConflictOfInterest`), `backend/app/models/__init__.py` (export), `backend/migrations/versions/0014_conflict_of_interest.py` (new), `backend/app/services/case_assignment_service.py` (workload sort + COI skip), `backend/app/services/authorization_service.py` (COI hiding in `scope_filter` + `can_access_application`), `backend/app/api/conflict_of_interest.py` (new), `backend/app/main.py` (router), `backend/tests/test_authz_matrix.py` (3 rows + read-before-write probe order), `backend/tests/test_assignment_coi.py` (new, 12), `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (4.1 ticked), `CONTEXT.md`, this log.
- API / schema / config / rule-file changes: **3 new routes** (`POST/GET /applications/{id}/conflicts-of-interest`, `POST /conflicts-of-interest/{id}/review`); **1 migration** (`0014_conflict_of_interest`, one table, no destructive change); **1 new rule file** (`assignment_policy.json`, labelled placeholder). No new permission — the routes reuse `CASE_READ` / `MONITORING_READ` the relevant roles already hold.
- Permission and audit effects: declaring is gated `require_any(CASE_READ, MONITORING_READ)` (an assigned scrutiny officer and a monitoring regional/supervisory officer may both declare); listing is the same gate; reviewing is `require(MONITORING_READ)` (only a monitoring/reviewer role resolves). Both write an audit event (`coi.declared`, `coi.reviewed`) with the before/after state.
- Decisions made: (1) **Hiding-only, never gating (DEC-0003, Appendix E line 412)** — a declaration removes the case from the declarer's view and pool; it is never a reason to refuse a transition, and a human reviewer still decides any exclusion or reassignment. There is no approved COI policy in the PRD, so inventing a substantive rule would violate the master prompt's "unverified regulatory values ship as labelled placeholder config" rule. (2) **Per-declarer, not case-wide** — one officer's declaration must not hide the case from every other officer; hiding is applied only in that declarer's own scope filter. (3) **Workload ordering is advisory** — the soft cap reorders, never drops, and jurisdiction is checked after the sort, so a placeholder formula cannot leave a queue empty or leak a case across regions. (4) **Declaring bypasses the declarer's own visibility check** — otherwise the act of declaring would make the case un-declarable, breaking re-declaration and the very recovery path the PRD wants. (5) **One open declaration per (case, declarant)** — enforced by a unique constraint plus an explicit check, so a retry is idempotent rather than silently creating a second hold. (6) No new DEC entry — this is the honest minimum behaviour the PRD already authorises (record + hide, human decides), built on the labelled-placeholder pattern already used in every prior phase; it does not introduce a new regulatory value, state or route.
- Verification commands and results:
  - `make lint` → "All checks passed!"
  - `make test` → **573 passed, 2 skipped** in 46.4s (up from 561; the +12 are the new `test_assignment_coi.py`). The 2 skips are the known Tesseract-absence OCR tests (BUG-0001) — skipped ≠ passed.
  - `SIH_DATABASE_URL=sqlite:////tmp/sih_check.db ../.venv/bin/alembic upgrade head` then `.../alembic check` → `No new upgrade operations detected.` (migration in sync with the model).
- Limitations: workload balancing is a single-process, in-request sort over active assignment counts — it is not a concurrency-safe reservation, so two simultaneous transitions could both pick the same least-loaded officer (harmless here: the case is still assigned, just not perfectly balanced). The COI "policy" is deliberately empty of substance: it records and hides, and defers every exclusion/reassignment decision to a human, exactly as Appendix E requires. Frontend has no COI panel yet — this task is backend-only (plan 4.1 does not list a UI); if a reviewer needs to resolve declarations in the demo, that is a separate small addition. `require_any(CASE_READ, MONITORING_READ)` means a monitoring officer who is not assigned the case can declare a COI on it, which matches the "monitoring sees across scope" model but is worth a human's eye.
- Open questions for a human: none blocking. Whether a supervisory/monitoring officer *should* be able to declare a conflict on a case they are not assigned (rather than only flagging it) is a policy choice the PRD leaves open — the current gate allows it, and it is easy to tighten to assigned-only if preferred.
- Next suggested task: **Phase 4.2** (plan line 73) — Annexure-2 checklist beside the documents (FR-13).

### IMPL-0030 — Phase 3.9: clock reconciliation (`reconcile.py` + `reconcile_clocks.py` job)
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-27 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / (this commit; gates green at this SHA)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 3 **3.9** (AGENT-08 deadlines/notifications, AGENT-09 reporting); PRD **§11** (the five-minute demo narrative, line 336: "admin changes a deadline visibly moves alerts" and the demo-clock escalation beat) and the plan's Phase 3 exit check (line 69: "reconciliation shows 0 diff"). Satisfies the plan line-68 task: "`reconcile_clocks.py` nightly job + report showing zero differences."
- Goal: give the demo *evidence* — not a claim — that nothing in the system stores a stale "due" flag, by recomputing every clock-derived date fresh from the current clock plus configuration and reporting whether it still equals what the live reader shows. A clean run prints `"zero_differences": true`, which is the report the demo shows after fast-forwarding the clock.
- What existed before: the entire deadline system already derived its dates on read — `deadline_service.collect_items` recomputes stage-ageing SLAs from `current_state_since + stage_sla_days`, `stages.due_dates` recomputes each active stage's due date from `started_at + days`, undertakings/EVC visits read their own stored-but-authoritative `due_at`/`scheduled_date`. Nothing stored a *derived* "due" flag anywhere, and that invariant was asserted piecemeal by the individual phase tests (3.3, 3.5, 3.6) but never proven across a whole live database in one place. There was no `reconcile.py`, no nightly job, no reconciliation endpoint.
- What I did (tests first):
  - **Service** — new `backend/app/services/reconcile.py`. `reconcile(db, now=None) -> ReconcileReport` recomputes, for every live clock, the date the deadline sweep would use and the date the independent reader would show, then diffs them. It checks: stage-ageing SLA (via `collect_items` + an independent `current_state_since + stage_sla_days` recompute), per-stage due dates (via `stages.due_dates` + an independent `started_at + days` recompute), undertaking `due_at`, and scheduled EVC visit `end_of_day(scheduled_date)`. It is **read-only** — it writes nothing, creates no alert, moves no case. `ReconcileReport` carries `now`, `clocks_checked`, `differences` (list of `ReconcileRow`), `frozen_alerts`, `frozen_notes`, and a `zero_differences` property; `as_dict()` renders the JSON the job and endpoint return.
  - **Frozen alert due_dates are reported, never counted as drift.** An `Alert` snapshots the cycle it fired in (its `dedupe_key` encodes `{state}:{entered_on}` for stage ageing); after a transition or a demo-clock jump a live recompute reflects the *current* cycle, which legitimately differs from that frozen snapshot. Calling that a bug would be wrong, so reconciliation surfaces each frozen alert's `due_at` under `frozen_alerts` and appends a note explaining why they are excluded from the drift count — transparency without a false positive.
  - **API** — new `GET /internal/reconcile-clocks` in `backend/app/api/alerts.py`, gated `require(ADMIN_USERS)` (same gate as the sibling `/internal/deadline-sweep`), returning `report.as_dict()`. Registered in the router; one matrix row added to `tests/test_authz_matrix.py`.
  - **Nightly job** — new `backend/scripts/reconcile_clocks.py`, mirroring the existing `scripts/retention.py` CLI shape. Runs `reconcile(db)` and prints the JSON report. Default exit is 0 even with differences (a logging cron should not page); `--fail-on-diff` exits 1 when `zero_differences` is false, which is what a monitoring cron should use.
  - **Backend tests** — new `tests/test_reconcile.py` (**9 tests**): reconciliation reports zero differences on the seeded database; it is read-only (alert and application counts unchanged); **it still reports zero after a demo-clock jump** (the Phase 3 exit check — because nothing stores a due flag, advancing the clock 30 days changes the live recomputation and the reported value identically); a seeded active stage's ageing clock agrees with its recomputation; an arbitrary edit to a stage's `started_at` does *not* trip a false positive (proving the comparison is honest — it only reports a difference when the two reads genuinely diverge, not that any edit is auto-caught); a frozen alert is surfaced in `frozen_alerts` and excluded from the drift count with an explanatory note; the endpoint is admin-only (403 for `scrutiny_officer`); the endpoint requires auth (401); the endpoint returns `zero_differences: true` for an administrator.
  - **Verification of the job end-to-end** — after `make seed` (which runs `create_all()` and brings the dev SQLite DB current with the 3.7 `alerts.snoozed_until` column), `python -m scripts.reconcile_clocks` printed `{"clocks_checked": 37, "difference_count": 0, "differences": [], "frozen_alerts": [], "frozen_alerts_checked": 0, "notes": [], "now": "2026-10-22T09:00:00+00:00", "zero_differences": true}`.
- Files changed: `backend/app/services/reconcile.py` (new), `backend/app/api/alerts.py` (reconcile endpoint + `reconcile` import), `backend/scripts/reconcile_clocks.py` (new), `backend/tests/test_authz_matrix.py` (1 row), `backend/tests/test_reconcile.py` (new, 9), `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (3.9 ticked), `CONTEXT.md`, this log.
- API / schema / config / rule-file changes: **1 new read-only endpoint** (`GET /internal/reconcile-clocks`, `ADMIN_USERS`); **no migration** (reconciliation reads existing tables only); **no new config or rule file** (it composes the existing `stage_limits.json`/`deadline_policy.json` through the same `policy.stage_sla_days` / `stages.due_dates` / `collect_items` paths the sweep already uses — one source of truth, no second clock).
- Permission and audit effects: new gate `ADMIN_USERS` on the one route (matrix-tested). Reconciliation is read-only and writes **no** audit event — there is nothing to audit when nothing changes; that is deliberate, not an omission.
- Decisions made: (1) **Reconcile the *live* clock, not the frozen alert snapshot** — an alert's stored `due_at` describes the cycle it fired in and legitimately differs after a transition; treating it as drift would be a false positive that makes the report untrustworthy. The frozen value is surfaced for transparency with an explanatory note instead. (2) **Compose the existing readers, never a second derivation** — reconciliation calls `collect_items`, `stages.due_dates` and `policy.stage_sla_days` themselves, so if a phase changes how a date is computed the reconciliation follows automatically and can never silently disagree with the sweep. (3) **Read-only, always** — reconciliation proves an invariant; it must never repair, recompute-in-place, or write, or it would be changing the thing it is checking. (4) **`ADMIN_USERS`, not a new permission** — reading every deadline across every institution is the same class of global operational visibility as the deadline-sweep job it sits beside, and it reuses a permission the administrator role already holds. (5) **Default exit 0, `--fail-on-diff` for monitoring** — a nightly logging job should report, not page; a monitoring cron opts into the strict exit. (6) No new DEC entry — this is operational verification tooling over already-labelled configuration, not a regulatory value or a new state, so it does not trip the rule-15 gate; it follows DEC-0003 (advisory, no gating).
- Verification commands and results:
  - `make lint` → "All checks passed!"
  - `make test` → **561 passed, 2 skipped** in 26.0s (up from 552; the +9 are the new `test_reconcile.py`). The 2 skips are the known Tesseract-absence OCR tests (BUG-0001) — skipped ≠ passed.
  - `cd backend && ../.venv/bin/python -m scripts.reconcile_clocks` → `zero_differences: true`, `clocks_checked: 37`, `difference_count: 0` against the freshly-seeded dev DB.
- Limitations: reconciliation recomputes the dates the *sweep* and the *stage reader* would report; it does not independently re-derive a date from raw source documents (e.g. it trusts a verified `lease_expiry` field rather than re-parsing the PDF), which is correct — the field is the system's source of truth once verified. It checks the four live clock kinds the sweep collects (stage ageing, stage due, undertaking, EVC visit) plus frozen alerts; it does not yet cover the appeal-window or security-deposit clocks from 3.5/3.6, which are advisory snapshots computed on a different path — a future reconciliation pass could include them. Single-process only, like the rest of the prototype.
- Environment note: the local dev SQLite DB (`backend/sih26262.db`, gitignored synthetic demo data) predated migration `0013_alert_snooze` — it had been created by `create_all()` without an `alembic_version` row, so `alembic upgrade head` could not apply cleanly (it tried to recreate existing tables). `make seed` re-ran `create_all()`, which added the missing column and made the script run clean. This is pre-existing local drift from the 3.7 task, not caused by this change; the test suite is unaffected (tests use a fresh temp DB).
- Open questions: none for this task. Whether the appeal/security-deposit advisory clocks should join the reconciliation pass is a small future addition, not a blocker.
- Next task: the Phase 3 exit check (plan line 69) — advance the demo clock → confirm alerts fire at their configured lead times → confirm escalation rises L0→L1→L2 → run reconciliation and show 0 diff. Then Phase 4 (scrutiny, deficiency, compliance; FR-06, FR-12–FR-15).

### IMPL-0029 — Phase 3.8: demo clock (`demo_clock.py` + `DemoClock.tsx`)
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-27 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / `9e99f9c` (feature commit; gates green at this SHA)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 3 **3.8** (AGENT-11 frontend integration, AGENT-08 for the service); PRD **§11** (line 334: "a demo clock that advances time to show reminders and escalation") and the five-minute demo narrative (line 336). Satisfies the plan line-67 task: "`demo_clock.py` endpoint + `DemoClock.tsx`; disabled when `SIH_ENV=production`; test that prod config refuses it."
- Goal: let a presenter jump the injectable clock forward so reminders and escalation can be *shown* without waiting for real days — and make it impossible for that control to move time in production, or to pretend to work on a deployment whose clock is not injectable.
- What existed before: `core/clock.py` already provided `SystemClock`/`FixedClock`/`OffsetClock`, a global `get_clock`/`set_clock`, and `build_clock()` reading `settings.clock_mode` (`system|fixed|offset`); `config.py` already special-cased a non-`system` `clock_mode` in production validation ("the injectable clock must not be fixed in production", line ~253) and documented `clock_mode` as "injectable, never ambient" (line ~87). Nothing exposed a way to *move* the clock at runtime. No `demo_clock.py`, no endpoint, no UI.
- What I did (tests first):
  - **Service** — new `backend/app/services/demo_clock.py`. `status_view()` (read-only; reports `enabled`, `production`, `mode`, `now`, and hides the panel when not enabled); `advance(db, access, days)` (fractional days allowed); `set_to(db, access, moment)` (explicit ISO-8601 instant); `reset(db, access)` (returns to the configured `SIH_CLOCK_FIXED_AT` baseline). Two hard guards sit at the top of every mutating path, before anything else runs: `_refuse_production()` raises `403` whenever `settings.is_production` is true, and `_require_fixed_clock()` raises `409` unless a `FixedClock` is actually installed — on a live `SystemClock` the control refuses rather than silently doing nothing. Every move is audited (`demo_clock.advance`/`.set`/`.reset`, entity `clock`/`demo`, with before/after instants and the actor) through `AuditService(db).append(...)`, and the service commits so the audit row and the clock change land together. `reset` re-reads the baseline from settings each time, so the panel returns to the deployment's own demo instant, never a hard-coded date; with no baseline configured it refuses loudly (`409`) instead of inventing an epoch.
  - **API** — new `backend/app/api/demo_clock.py`, a `router = APIRouter(prefix="/admin/demo-clock", tags=["admin"])` with `GET ""` (status), `POST /advance` (`AdvanceRequest{days: float}`), `POST /set` (`SetRequest{moment: str}`), `POST /reset`. All four gated by `require(ADMIN_USERS)` — moving time re-ages every case and fires every reminder, so only an administrator may do it. Registered in `app/main.py`'s router list. Request/response models use `ConfigDict(extra="forbid")` so a stray field is a 422, not a silent no-op.
  - **Authz matrix** — four rows added to `test_authz_matrix.py`: `GET /admin/demo-clock` and the three `POST`s all require `ADMIN_USERS` and nothing less.
  - **Backend tests** — new `tests/test_demo_clock.py` (12 tests): status reports the pinned demo instant; advance moves time forward and is audited; fractional days work; reset returns to the baseline; set accepts an explicit instant and rejects a malformed one (422); a non-admin (`scrutiny_officer`) is denied by the router (403); an unauthenticated caller is 401; **production refuses to move time even for an administrator** (the hard guard, by monkeypatching `get_settings` to a production-shaped `Settings`); advance refuses when no `FixedClock` is installed (409); reset without a configured baseline refuses (409). Expected instants use `iso()` (UTC, `Z`-suffixed) so assertions match the API's own serialisation. An autouse fixture restores the pinned `FixedClock` after every test so a moved clock cannot leak into another test.
  - **Frontend** — new `frontend/src/components/DemoClock.tsx`: a permission-reachable admin panel that shows the current instant, offers plain-language shortcuts ("Jump 1 day / 3 days / 1 week") and "Reset to the start", and *renders a "switched off" message* (rather than a broken control) when the API reports `enabled: false` — which is exactly what production or a non-injectable deployment returns. Wired into `App.tsx` as a new `demo` hash view gated on `isAdmin` (`permissions.includes("admin.users")`); nav button uses the existing `clock` icon. `types.ts` gains `DemoClockStatus`/`DemoClockMove`; `api.ts` gains `demoClockStatus`/`demoClockAdvance`/`demoClockSet`/`demoClockReset`.
- Files changed: `backend/app/services/demo_clock.py` (new), `backend/app/api/demo_clock.py` (new), `backend/app/main.py` (router import + registration), `backend/tests/test_authz_matrix.py` (4 rows), `backend/tests/test_demo_clock.py` (new), `frontend/src/components/DemoClock.tsx` (new), `frontend/src/App.tsx` (view + nav + isAdmin), `frontend/src/types.ts` (+2 types), `frontend/src/api.ts` (+4 methods). No model, migration or rule-file changes.
- API/schema/config changes: four new admin-only endpoints under `/api/v1/admin/demo-clock`. Config already existed (`SIH_CLOCK_MODE`, `SIH_CLOCK_FIXED_AT`); nothing new added.
- Permission and audit effects: new gate `ADMIN_USERS` on all four routes (matrix-tested); every clock move writes a `demo_clock.*` audit event with actor and before/after instants. The production refusal and the not-injectable refusal are 403/409 with a plain message and no audit row (there is nothing to audit when nothing moved).
- Decisions made: (1) **Refuse, never pretend** — when the clock is not injectable or the deployment is production, the service raises rather than silently doing nothing or half-working; a "demo clock" that quietly fails is worse than one that says it is off. (2) **Production guard is a hard check at the top of every mutating path**, not a UI affordance — the frontend hiding the button is a convenience, the 403 is the actual safety. (3) **`reset` re-reads the baseline from settings** rather than a constant, so each deployment returns to its own demo instant. (4) **`ADMIN_USERS`, not a new permission** — moving time is the same class of administrative action as managing assignments, and it reuses a permission the administrator role already holds. (5) No new DEC entry — this is operational demo tooling, not a regulatory value or a new state, so it does not trip the rule-15 gate; it follows the existing "demo clock is injectable, never ambient" convention already recorded in `config.py`.
- Verification commands and results:
  - `make lint` → "All checks passed!"
  - `make test` → **552 passed, 2 skipped** in 24.8s (up from 540; the +12 are the new `test_demo_clock.py`). The 2 skips are the known Tesseract-absence OCR tests (BUG-0001).
  - `cd frontend && npm run build` → `tsc --noEmit` clean, vite build succeeded ("✓ built in 344ms", 52 modules).
- Limitations: the demo clock moves the *global* fixed clock for the whole process, so it is a demo/narrative tool, not a per-case time travel; on a multi-replica deployment it would only move the replica that handled the request (the prototype is single-process). The `offset` clock mode is not exposed through this control (only `fixed`), because the panel's job is the five-minute demo narrative. Not verified against a running multi-replica stack — single-process only.
- Open questions: none for this task. Whether a *reviewer* (non-admin) should ever see a read-only clock display is an AGENT-11 UX question, not needed for 3.8.
- Next task: Phase 3.9 `reconcile_clocks.py` nightly job + report showing zero differences, then the Phase 3 exit check (advance demo clock → alerts fire → escalate L0→L1→L2; reconciliation 0 diff).

### IMPL-0028 — Phase 3.7: snooze-once-with-reason on alerts (PRD §11 line 293)
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-27 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / (this commit)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 3 **3.7** (AGENT-08 deadlines/notifications); PRD **§11** (line 293: "One snooze with a reason, no second"); the alert-fatigue mitigation table (line 348: "Alert fatigue | Digest option, snooze once with reason, escalate only past due").
- Goal: let an officer hold a nagging reminder for a short, explicit window — exactly once, and only with a recorded reason — so the one-snooze limit is enforced by the data and the hold is auditable, without ever letting a snooze silently retire an alert.
- What existed before: the `Alert` model already carried the full acknowledge/close lifecycle (`acknowledged_at`/`acknowledged_by`/`closed_at`/`closed_by`/`close_reason`) and `alert_service.deliver` already suppressed a reminder whenever `status != "open"` (so an acknowledged or closed alert is never re-nagged); `POST /alerts/{id}/ack` and `POST /alerts/{id}/close` existed. There was no snooze: no `snoozed_until`, no reason, no endpoint, and no way to pause a reminder without closing it.
- What I did (tests first):
  - **Model** — `Alert` gains three columns: `snoozed_until` (nullable timestamp; non-NULL is the "already snoozed" signal, so the one-snooze limit is enforced by the data and needs no extra flag), `snooze_reason` (mandatory free text, same shape as `close_reason`), `snoozed_by` (FK to `users`).
  - **Migration** — new `backend/migrations/versions/0013_alert_snooze.py` adding the three columns with a named FK `fk_alerts_snoozed_by` (rewritten by hand from the autogenerate draft to match the repo's docstring/revision style and to drop the spurious `drop_constraint(None)` the draft emitted). `alembic upgrade head` applies it; `alembic check` → "No new upgrade operations detected".
  - **Delivery** — `alert_service.deliver` gains a snooze suppression branch: while `snoozed_until` is still in the future the reminder is suppressed exactly like an acknowledged/closed one (returns `provider_ref="suppressed"`, `suppressed=True`). A snooze is a **temporary hold, not a lifecycle terminal**: the endpoint deliberately leaves `status` untouched, so once the clock passes `snoozed_until` the alert is eligible to be delivered again (it was never closed). This is the one behaviour difference from ack/close, which are permanent.
  - **API** — new `POST /alerts/{alert_id}/snooze` (`require(MONITORING_READ)`, same gate as ack/close); a `SnoozeRequest` payload (`reason` mandatory — a blank reason is a 422, same teaching as close; `days` 1–30, defaulted to 1, so the hold length is explicit and bounded rather than a silent fixed window). "No second": if `snoozed_until` is already set the handler answers with the **current** state (idempotent read) instead of extending the hold or replacing the reason, so a retry is safe and the caller can see when the existing hold expires. Writes an `alert.snoozed` audit event recording who, why, and until when (before/after both carry the unchanged status plus the new hold). `GET /alerts` and `_serialise_alert` expose the three snooze fields.
  - **Authz** — matrix row for the new route (`POST /alerts/{id}/snooze` → `MONITORING_READ`) plus a JSON probe body, so the derived-route coverage test accepts it.
  - **Tests** — `backend/tests/test_alert_snooze.py` (**5 tests**). Snooze records the fields and audits: status is unchanged (a snooze is not a terminal), `snoozed_until`/`snooze_reason`/`snoozed_by` are set, an `alert.snoozed` audit event carries the reason. A blank reason is refused (422). A second snooze is refused, not extended: the second call's `snoozed_until` equals the first's and the original reason is not replaced. Delivery is suppressed while snoozed and resumes after expiry: an `"open"` alert is snoozed for 1 day (the sweep leaves seeded alerts `"delivered"`, so the test puts one back to `"open"` to test the nag path), `deliver` is suppressed/un-delivered while the window is live, and after the clock passes `snoozed_until` `deliver` is eligible again. The route needs a token (401).
- Files changed: `backend/app/models/compliance.py` (three columns), `backend/migrations/versions/0013_alert_snooze.py` (new), `backend/app/services/alert_service.py` (snooze suppression in `deliver`, + `to_utc`/`get_clock` imports), `backend/app/api/alerts.py` (snooze endpoint, `SnoozeRequest`, `timedelta` import, list + serialise fields), `backend/tests/test_alert_snooze.py` (new, 5), `backend/tests/test_authz_matrix.py` (matrix row + probe body), `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (3.7 ticked), `README.md` + `CONTEXT.md`, this log.
- Migrations / API / config / rule-file changes: **1 migration** (`0013_alert_snooze`, three additive nullable/defaulted columns — no backfill needed, existing alerts simply have no hold); **1 new endpoint** (`POST /alerts/{id}/snooze`, `MONITORING_READ`); **no new config/rule file** (the 1–30-day bound is a payload constraint, not a regulatory value, so it needs no placeholder labelling). No UI this task (AGENT-11 adopts the endpoint separately).
- **Permission and audit effects:** no new permissions — the route reuses the existing `MONITORING_READ` closure that already gates ack/close. The snooze writes one `alert.snoozed` audit event (who, why, until when), matching how ack/close are audited. A snooze never closes, acknowledges or retires an alert — it only suppresses the *next* delivery attempt while the window is live, and it is always reversible by the clock.
- Decisions made: (1) **A snooze is temporary, not terminal** — the endpoint leaves `status` alone and only sets `snoozed_*`; this is why an expired snooze makes the alert deliverable again, unlike ack/close which are permanent. The first draft set `status="snoozed"`, which would have blocked future delivery forever (the bug the delivery test caught); corrected before commit. (2) **"No second" is enforced by data, not a counter** — `snoozed_until IS NOT NULL` is the signal, and the handler answers with the current state rather than erroring, so a retry is idempotent and a caller cannot extend a hold. (3) **The reason is mandatory** (422 on blank), matching the close-reason rule — a hold that silences a reminder with no record is indistinguishable from one nobody looked at. (4) **The hold length is caller-supplied and bounded (1–30 days)** — the PRD gives no window figure, so it is an explicit, bounded request parameter rather than a hard-coded silent default; this is an internal policy choice, not a regulatory value, so it needs no placeholder labelling. (5) No new DEC entry — follows the existing ack/close audit pattern and the §11 "one snooze with a reason" mandate directly.
- Verification: exact commands + results:
  - `pytest tests/test_alert_snooze.py -q` → **5 passed**, 1 warning (first run failed 2: the delivery test's `alert` object was stale after the API committed in another session — `expire_on_commit=False` plus the identity map — fixed with `db.refresh(alert)`; and the first draft wrongly set `status="snoozed"`, which the delivery test caught and I corrected before commit).
  - `make lint` → All checks passed!
  - `alembic upgrade head` + `alembic check` (against a scratch SQLite DB) → migration applies, "No new upgrade operations detected".
  - `make test` → **540 passed, 2 skipped, 1 warning in 30.53s** (the 2 skips are the unchanged OCR/Tesseract-absent workaround, BUG-0001). +5 vs the 535 baseline at bd7e632. Existing `test_alerts.py`, `test_authz_matrix.py` and the sweep tests pass unchanged, confirming the snooze branch is additive (an alert with no `snoozed_until` takes the same path as before).
  - Not run: `npm run build` (no frontend change), Compose/Postgres (SQLite only).
- Status per requirement touched: §11 "one snooze with a reason, no second" — **Tested**; alert-fatigue "snooze once with reason" (line 348) — **Tested**.
- Limitations / assumptions / manual steps: the 1–30-day hold bound is an internal policy choice (no PRD figure); if AICTE later specifies a snooze window it becomes a config value, not a code change. A snooze suppresses the *next* delivery attempt only — it does not cancel an already-sent alert (a delivered alert stays delivered). Not verified against Postgres/Compose (SQLite only). No UI this task.
- Related bugs: none logged as a BUG entry — the stale-object and wrong-status issues were caught by the new tests during the task and fixed before commit (not regressions in shipped code).
- Open questions for a human: (1) Should the snooze window be a configured value (like the deadline offsets) once AICTE specifies one, rather than a per-request parameter? (2) Should a snoozed alert still count as "open" in the monitoring/pendency counts, or be visibly distinguished? — currently it stays `"open"` and shows its `snoozed_until`.
- Next suggested task: Phase **3.8** — `demo_clock.py` endpoint + `DemoClock.tsx`; disabled when `SIH_ENV=production`; test that prod config refuses it (per the implementation plan's order).

### IMPL-0027 — Phase 3.6: security-deposit clock (FR-33) as labelled placeholder + penalty schedule
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-27 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / (this commit)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 3 **3.6** (AGENT-02 workflow hardening); PRD **FR-33** (line 213: "Security Deposit: 15-day clock for proof + Affidavit 3; 10% penalty to 31 May, 20% to 31 July; flag withdrawal after", APH cl. 2.25d-f); PRD **§7** rules table (line 244: "Security Deposit proof + Affidavit 3 | 15 days; 10% penalty to 31 May; 20% to 31 July; withdrawn after | 2.25f"); PRD **§11** (line 289: institution-side "Security Deposit due" cadence "Day 1, 10, 14; 31 May, 31 July", escalated to the Bureau).
- Goal: make "which penalty, and when" for an unpaid Security Deposit configuration rather than code, so the 15-day proof window, the 10%/20% penalty percentages, the fixed 31-May/31-July dates and the withdrawal marker are human-editable, clearly-labelled placeholders that compute and display a penalty band but never withdraw a case — with the actual withdrawal deferred to the rule-15 human gate.
- What existed before: the grant stage (`stage_graph.json`) was labelled unreachable in the prototype and `stage_limits.json` deferred the SD 15-day clock to this task; the `Undertaking` model (`obligation_key` + `due_at` + evidence + status) already existed and could carry the "proof + Affidavit 3" obligation with the day-1/10/14 reminders via the `undertaking_due` ladder, but no SD penalty schedule, no `security_deposit.py`, no `security_deposit_policy.json` and no SD endpoint existed. FR-33's figures lived only as PRD text.
- What I did (tests first):
  - **Config** — new `backend/rules/security_deposit_policy.json`: status `unverified` + `placeholder` + `advisory_only`, `source` citing FR-33 / §7 line 244 / §11 line 289 / APH cl. 2.25f; `proof_window_days: 15`; `obligation` (id `security_deposit_proof`, evidence `bank_receipt`, note that the 15-day window rides the `undertaking_due` ladder rather than a second clock); `penalty_schedule` (`sd_penalty_10` 10% `on_or_before` `05-31`/warning, `sd_penalty_20` 20% `on_or_before` `07-31`/critical), each cited to §7 line 244; `withdrawal_flag` (`after` `07-31`/escalated, note that it sets a computed marker for display only and never withdraws the case — rule 15); `reminder_thresholds` (day 1/10/14) documenting that they ride the `undertaking_due` ladder.
  - **Loader** — `policy.security_deposit_policy()` (`@lru_cache`, same as every other rules file).
  - **Service** — new `backend/app/services/security_deposit.py`: `proof_window_days()`; `penalty_for_date(on)` (pure date maths — walks the configured schedule in order and returns the first band whose `on_or_before` date is on/after `on`; a date after every band AND after the withdrawal `after` date reports `withdrawal_flagged: True` with no penalty percent — the honest answer, never a guess; the fixed `MM-DD` dates are calendar-anchored so the band is the same every year); `proof_deadline(application)` (15 days from `current_state_since` when approved — the same honest-proxy anchor as the appeal clock until a first-class grant event exists; None when not approved); `sd_snapshot(db, application, now)` (the advisory read-only view combining the deadline, the penalty band for today, the `withdrawal_flagged` marker and the SD undertaking's status; a non-approved case reports `status: "not_applicable"`). Reads the SD `Undertaking` by `obligation_key` `security_deposit_proof` when one exists.
  - **API** — new `GET /applications/{application_id}/security-deposit` (`require_any(CASE_READ, APPLICATION_READ)`, i.e. `READ_ANY`, mirroring `appeal-window`/`escalation`/`lease-check`); authz-matrix row added; read-only, no audit (nothing changes).
  - **Tests** — `backend/tests/test_security_deposit.py` (**21 tests**). Config contract (6): loads `unverified`+`placeholder`+`advisory_only`; cites FR-33 + APH cl. 2.25f; window is 15 days; schedule is 10 then 20 on `05-31`/`07-31`; withdrawal flag is after `07-31`; file is readable JSON in the rules dir. Penalty date maths (6): a date in May → 10%; 31 May (inclusive boundary) → 10%; a date in June → 20%; 31 July (inclusive) → 20%; 1 August → withdrawal marker with **no** penalty percent; the dates are calendar-anchored (same band in 2027). Snapshot (4): a submitted (unapproved) case is `not_applicable`; an approved case reports the 15-day proof deadline; the snapshot reflects today's penalty band (2026-10-22 is past 31 July → withdrawal marker set, no penalty); the snapshot is **advisory and writes nothing** (no version bump, no state change, no audit event). Undertaking (1): when the SD proof undertaking exists, its status rides the snapshot. API (4): the endpoint serves the snapshot to a signed-in user; 401 without a token; respects application scope (own case 200, another institution's 403/404); an open window is reported for an approved case. The approve-driving tests use the real `approve` transition (scrutiny officer, reason only) pinned to the fixed clock so the 15-day deadline is deterministic.

Window maths (4): a case in the queue (submitted) has **no window**; a decided case has a 7-day window opening/closing end-of-day in the business tz from `current_state_since`; the window is `lapsed` (and `may_file: false`) after the close; the snapshot flags `may_file` only while open and carries `placeholder`/`advisory_only`. One-appeal limit (2): a recorded `file_appeal` transition is counted by action; the `may_file` flag flips off once `appeals_filed == max_appeals`. Sweep clock (3): the sweep raises the day-3 and day-6 reminders at the right instants with `placeholder`/`window_days` on the payload; re-running the sweep creates nothing new (`dedupe_key`) and leaves exactly one alert row for the case/level; an undecided case gets **no** appeal alert. API (4): the endpoint serves the snapshot to a signed-in user; 401 without a token; respects application scope (own case 200, another institution's 403/404); an open window is reported for a decided case. Advisory (1): the snapshot is read-only and writes nothing (no version bump, no state change, no audit event). The sweep-driving tests decide the case via the real `reject` transition (reason-only, no documents/rules) and pin `current_state_since` so the window anchor is deterministic.

### IMPL-0024 — Phase 3.4: escalation chain (L0–L3) as labelled placeholder + §11 percentage/unassigned rules
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-27 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / e2fa615 (work uncommitted at implementation)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 3 **3.4** (AGENT-02 workflow hardening); PRD **§11** (lines 274-293: the escalation chain L0 assignee → L1 Regional officer → L2 Regulation Bureau → L3 competent authority, and the trigger table — near stage limit 70%/90% → L1 at 100%, L2 at 125%; unassigned in queue → Regional after 1 working day, L2 after 3 days); PRD **line 12** (§11 operational thresholds — 70/90/100/125%, the 1-/3-day escalation, alert lead times — are project proposals, not AICTE rules); **§19 Q5** (line 357: "Who is the designated authority at each escalation level, by Region?" — open question).
- Goal: make "who a missed or nearly-missed clock is escalated to" configuration rather than code, so the chain, the near-limit ladder and the unassigned rules are human-editable, clearly-labelled placeholders that never gate a case.
- What existed before: `alert_service.recipients_for(db, application, severity)` resolved recipients **by alert severity** (warning/critical/escalated) — regional officers from warning up, supervisory officer at escalated — but there was no L0-L3 chain, no percentage ladder (the ageing sweep used offset-days from `deadline_policy.json.stage_ageing`, not a share of the limit), no "unassigned in queue" rule, and no `supervisory_officers` public helper. `monitoring_service.escalations()` merely listed already-fired `level=="escalated"` alerts; it did not compute a chain level. Nothing mapped the PRD's Regulation Bureau / competent authority to any role.
- What I did (tests first):
  - **Tests** — `backend/tests/test_escalation.py` (**35 tests**). Config contract (10): loads `unverified`+`placeholder`; cites §11 + the project-proposal caveat/§19 Q5; chain is the ordered L0→L3 with the PRD's own labels; every entry has a `recipient_kind` and a `source`; the ladder is exactly 70/90/100/125 in order mapping L0/L0/L1/L2; unassigned rules are 1-day→L1 then 3-day→L2; **L2 is a labelled placeholder** (`supervisory_officer`, `placeholder: true`, explanatory note — §19 Q5 open); **L3 is `unmapped`** (no competent-authority role exists → resolves to nobody, never an invented authority); the file is readable JSON in the rules dir. Percentage ladder (7): below 70% → None; 70/90 → L0; 100 → L1; 125 → L2; highest crossed threshold wins; a 120% case (the seeded existing case: 12 days in a 10-day stage) is L1; a missing/zero limit answers None, never a guess. Unassigned rules (4): 0 days → None; 1 → L1; 3 and 5 → L2; **working-day arithmetic skips weekends** (Mon→Thu = 3, Fri→Mon = 1) and **uses the configured calendar**, not a hard-coded weekend (a no-weekend calendar counts every day). Recipients (5): L0 → the case's assigned officer; L1 → the region's `regional_officers`; L2 → the region's `supervisory_officers` (placeholder mapping still resolves the seeded officer); **L3 → nobody**; an unknown level → nobody. Snapshot (5): the seeded existing case reports 10/12/120%/L1 + recipients; the snapshot is **advisory and writes nothing** (no version bump, no state change, no audit event); the seeded unassigned case (region south, 2 working days in queue) reports `unassigned: true`, 2 working days, L1; an assigned case is not flagged unassigned; a stage with no configured limit reports no percentage and no level. Sweep wiring (1): the stage-ageing alert payload carries `escalation_level` alongside `sla_days`/`days_in_state` on the sweep's own clock. API (3): `GET /applications/{id}/escalation` serves the snapshot + chain to a signed-in user; 401 without a token; **respects application scope** (the college sees its own case, another institution's is 403/404).
  - **Config** — new `backend/rules/escalation_chain.json`: status `unverified` + `placeholder` + `advisory_only`, `source` citing §11 lines 274-293 + line 12 + §19 Q5; the `chain` (L0 assignee/`assignee`, L1 Regional officer/`regional_officer` — a direct role match, L2 Regulation Bureau/`supervisory_officer` **labelled placeholder**, L3 competent authority/`unmapped` **deliberate gap**), each entry `source`-labelled; `percentage_thresholds` (70→L0, 90→L0, 100→L1, 125→L2) with per-rung PRD citations; `unassigned_in_queue` (1 working day→L1, 3→L2). The chain maps **roles, not named people** — §19 Q5 is open, so nothing invents an authority.
  - **Loader** — `policy.escalation_chain_policy()` (`@lru_cache`, same as every other rules file).
  - **Service** — new `backend/app/services/escalation.py`: `chain()`, `level_for_percentage(elapsed, limit)` (pure; None when no limit is configured), `unassigned_level(working_days)` (pure), `working_days_between(start, end, cfg)` (configured calendar, never a hard-coded weekend), `recipients_for_level(db, application, level)` (resolves by the level's `recipient_kind`; unmapped/unknown → nobody), and `escalation_snapshot(db, application, now)` (the advisory read-only view combining the percentage level and the unassigned level). Reuses `alert_service.regional_officers` and a **new** `alert_service.supervisory_officers` (added so the L2 region-scoped supervisory query is not duplicated), plus `policy.stage_sla_days` (the 3.3 limit) and `working_calendar` — no second clock.
  - **API** — new `GET /applications/{application_id}/escalation` (`require_any(CASE_READ, APPLICATION_READ)`, i.e. `READ_ANY`, mirroring `lease-check`/`blocked-flags`); authz-matrix row added; read-only, no audit (nothing changes).
  - **Minimal sweep wiring** — the stage-ageing `DeadlineItem.payload` gains `escalation_level` (computed from the **same** `elapsed`/`sla` the sweep already had), so a fired ageing alert shows the L0-L3 rung it reached. Additive only: the alert set, `dedupe_key` and recipients are untouched (existing `test_alerts.py` idempotence/recipient tests still pass unchanged).
- Files changed: `backend/rules/escalation_chain.json` (new), `backend/app/services/policy.py`, `backend/app/services/alert_service.py` (new `supervisory_officers`), `backend/app/services/escalation.py` (new), `backend/app/services/deadline_service.py` (payload enrichment), `backend/app/api/workflow.py` (new endpoint), `backend/tests/test_escalation.py` (new, 35), `backend/tests/test_authz_matrix.py` (matrix row), `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (3.4 ticked), `README.md` + `CONTEXT.md`, this log.
- Migrations / API / config / rule-file changes: **no migration** (no model change — the level is computed, never stored); **1 new endpoint** (`GET /applications/{id}/escalation`, read-only, `READ_ANY`); **1 new rule file** (`escalation_chain.json`); the sweep's stage-ageing payload gains one additive key. No UI this task (AGENT-11 adopts the endpoint separately).
- **Permission and audit effects:** no new permissions and no new gates — the endpoint reuses the existing `READ_ANY` closure; the escalation snapshot and the sweep enrichment are advisory (DEC-0003) and write no audit event. Escalation computes who *would* be notified; it never moves, gates or rejects a case, and the existing alert/audit behaviour is unchanged.
- Decisions made: (1) **Chain maps roles, not named authorities** — §19 Q5 ("designated authority per level, by Region") is open, so L1/L2 resolve to seeded roles and L3 is `unmapped` (zero recipients) rather than an invented person. (2) **L2 → `supervisory_officer` is a labelled placeholder** — the PRD's "Regulation Bureau" has no explicit role; the nearest region-scoped role is used and the placeholder is marked in config + note, not asserted as fact. (3) **Percentages ride the sweep's existing clock** — `level_for_percentage(days_in_state, sla)` uses the same elapsed/limit the ageing item already carried; no second time source. (4) **Minimal, additive sweep wiring** — only the payload gains a key; alert set, dedupe and recipients are untouched so no idempotence/recipient test changes. (5) **Advisory snapshot, no gating** — follows DEC-0003; the endpoint is read-only and writes nothing. (6) **`supervisory_officers` added as a public helper** mirroring `regional_officers`, so L2 resolution is not a duplicated private query. (7) No new DEC entry — follows DEC-0003 (advisory, no gating) and the placeholder rule already logged with 2.4/3.2/3.3.
- Verification: exact commands + results:

The chain, the percentage ladder and the unassigned rules are all configuration; `escalation.py` provides the pure lookups and the advisory snapshot; the sweep's ageing alerts carry the level; the endpoint serves it read-only.
