### IMPL-0023 — Phase 3.3: stage limits as labelled placeholder day counts + per-stage due dates
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-27 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / 956495a (work uncommitted at implementation)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 3 **3.3** (AGENT-02 workflow hardening); PRD **§19 Q1** (line 353: "Target days for Scrutiny, Re-Scrutiny, EVC scheduling, SHC, Executive Committee?" — open question for AICTE) and PRD line 12 (operational thresholds are project proposals, not AICTE rules; Unverified rules stay advisory and never block, return or reject); §11 ageing bands (0-7/8-15/16-30/30+) consume the resulting clocks.
- Goal: give every PRD stage a clearly-labelled placeholder day count in its own configuration file, and derive per-stage due dates on read — so the ageing/escalation clocks of 3.4+ have one honest, human-editable source of truth that never pretends to be official.
- What existed before: day counts rode **inline and unlabelled** in `workflow_routes.json` (`stage_sla_days` per route, keyed by *state*), consumed by `policy.stage_sla_days()` → `deadline_service.collect_items` (stage-ageing alerts), `monitoring_service`, `reporting_service` and the EVC booking path; no per-stage due dates existed anywhere (a stage row knew its start but not its target); no test pinned the values, and the file's only label was a blanket "illustrative configuration" note.
- What I did (tests first):
  - **Tests** — `backend/tests/test_stage_limits.py` (**15 tests**). Config contract (7): loads `unverified`+`placeholder`; cites §19 Q1 (the open question that keeps it a placeholder); **every route lists exactly the stages of its own stage graph** (cross-config — limits may not invent or omit a stage); day counts are positive ints or explicitly null-with-a-note; per-state overrides may only name states the stage owns; **unmapped-state counts may only name states the route defines but no stage owns** (today `sac_review` — 3.1's open mapping question, carried honestly: the clock is about the state, without inventing the stage label); `workflow_routes.json` no longer carries `stage_sla_days` (one source of truth); the file is readable JSON in the rules dir. Behaviour (2): **all 14 pre-3.3 (route, state) → days pairs pinned** so the relocation provably changes nothing; unknown route/state and limit-less states (`returned`, `draft`) answer `None`, never a default. Due dates (6): an active stage gets `started_at + configured days`; the count follows the state the case is actually in (the EVC 30/10 booked-vs-held override); pending and completed stages have no due date (clock starts on entry, stops on completion); a `days: null` stage has no due date **and carries the note that explains why**; the view is computed twice with identical results and stores nothing (no version bump, no state change, no new column); the view covers the whole stage list in order so a dashboard can render it.
  - **Config** — new `backend/rules/stage_limits.json`: per-route, per-PRD-stage day counts (all 8 stages × 4 routes), every entry `source`-labelled as a placeholder citing §19 Q1; `days: null` entries carry a note saying *why* there is no clock (stage unreachable in the prototype state machine / the compliance cycle waits on the institution / the appeal and SD clocks are separate Phase 3.5/3.6 work, not stage ageing); the EVC stage keeps its two-count difference as a `state_overrides` block (30 days to hold a booked visit, 10 for the decision after it was held — moved unchanged); `unmapped_state_days: {evc_sac_shc: {sac_review: 20}}` with a note tying it to 3.1's open mapping question. **The 14 values moved unchanged** from `workflow_routes.json` (5/15/30/10 etc.) — the change is labelling and location, not numbers, and the pinned test proves it.
  - **Config cleanup** — the four inline `stage_sla_days` blocks removed from `workflow_routes.json` (one source of truth; the test that forbids them keeps it that way).
  - **Loader** — `policy.stage_limits_policy()` (`@lru_cache`, same as every other rules file).
  - **Service** — `policy.stage_sla_days(route_key, state)` rewired to the new file: the count belongs to the PRD stage the state maps to (via `stage_graph.json`'s `active_states`), state overrides win where configured, unmapped states fall back to `unmapped_state_days`, and everything else answers `None` — same signature, same values, so its four consumers (deadline sweep, monitoring, reporting, EVC) changed behaviour not at all. New `stages.due_dates(db, application)`: per-stage `days`/`due_at`/`placeholder`/`note` derived on read from the stage rows + config — active stages only (the clock starts on entry and stops on completion), state override applied when the case sits in that state, nothing persisted (same principle as the deadline clocks and the FR-03 evaluations: no stored verdicts).
- Files changed: `backend/rules/stage_limits.json` (new), `backend/rules/workflow_routes.json` (stage_sla_days blocks removed), `backend/app/services/policy.py`, `backend/app/services/stages.py`, `backend/tests/test_stage_limits.py` (new, 15), `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (3.3 ticked), `README.md` + `CONTEXT.md`, this log.
- Migrations / API / config / rule-file changes: **no migration** (no model change — due dates are computed, never stored); **no new endpoint** (`stages.due_dates()` is the API surface for AGENT-11 to render; the sweep's stage-ageing alerts already flow through `stage_sla_days`); **1 new rule file** (`stage_limits.json`) and **1 existing rule file edited** (`workflow_routes.json` slas removed). No permission, audit, transition or alert behaviour changed — the same alerts fire on the same schedule.
- **Permission and audit effects:** none. The day counts are advisory configuration (they alert and age, they never gate); no transition reads them; no new audit events (alerts keep their existing `alert.fired`/`alert.escalated` events, unchanged).
- Decisions made: (1) **A dedicated file, not more inline blocks** — the plan names `stage_limits.json`; the counts are operational thresholds that AICTE must be able to review in one place, clearly labelled, without reading route definitions. (2) **Stage-keyed with state overrides** — the stage graph maps one stage to possibly several states that count differently (EVC booked vs held); keying purely by stage would lose that, keying purely by state would hide the stage the clock belongs to. (3) **`sac_review` keeps its 20 days under `unmapped_state_days`** — the state exists and ages, but no PRD stage owns it; the config is honest about the state without inventing the stage label (3.1's open question, carried forward). (4) **Values moved unchanged** — this task is labelling and structure, not new numbers; inventing "better" placeholder days would be inventing regulatory targets. (5) **Due dates computed, never stored** — consistent with the repo's no-stored-verdicts principle; changing a day count updates every due date at once with no stale column. (6) **Null limits are explicit and explained** — a stage with no clock says so with a note, so silence is never mistaken for "no problem". (7) No new DEC entry — follows DEC-0003 (advisory, no gating) and the placeholder rule already logged with 2.4.
- Verification: exact commands + results:
  - `pytest tests/test_stage_limits.py -q -p no:randomly` → **15 passed** (first run; one test fixed afterwards — see below).
  - `make lint` → first run flagged `SIM102` (nested `if` in `due_dates`) → combined into a single condition; then `All checks passed!`.
  - First `make test` → **1 failed / 456 passed**: `test_due_dates_are_computed_on_read_and_store_nothing` compared a SQLite-round-tripped naive `datetime` against the tz-aware fixed-clock value (SQLite stores no timezone; the same instant, different repr) — fixed by comparing through `to_utc`, the same normalization the service itself uses; also fixed a SAWarning in the same test (`db.get(StageInstance, None)` on an unseeded stage's NULL id — the assertion now looks up the seeded scrutiny row).
  - `make lint && make test` → **457 passed, 1 warning in 46.34s** (0 skipped; +15 vs 442)
  - Not run: `alembic` (no model change), `npm run build` (no frontend change), Compose/Postgres (SQLite only).
- Status per requirement touched: Plan 3.3 "`stage_limits.json` with **placeholder** day counts, clearly labelled; per-stage due dates" — **Implemented · Tested**. Every count is a labelled placeholder citing §19 Q1; `stages.due_dates()` derives per-stage due dates on read; the ageing sweep's inputs moved without changing a single value.
- Limitations / assumptions / manual steps:
  - **All day counts are placeholders** (5/15/30/10/20 as configured in Phase 1); AICTE's §19 Q1 answer replaces them via config edit + review — no code change.
  - `stages.due_dates()` has no endpoint yet; AGENT-11 renders it (the deadline sweep's alerts already flow through the same config).
  - The compliance cycle, SHC, Executive Committee, Appeal and Grant stages deliberately have **no clock** (`days: null` + note); the appeal 7-day window and the SD 15-day clock are separate Phase 3.5/3.6 work, not stage ageing.
  - Re-entering a stage (Re-Scrutiny) restarts its clock from the new stint's `started_at` (3.1 semantics); earlier stints' due dates are not reconstructed (their times live in audit).
  - Not verified against Postgres/Compose; SQLite only.
- Related bugs: none logged as BUG-#### (the two test fixes were environment/quirk issues in my own new test, caught and fixed within the task; no product behaviour regressed).
- Open questions for a human: (1) **§19 Q1 — the official target days** for Scrutiny/Re-Scrutiny/EVC scheduling/SHC/Executive Committee (carried from the PRD; recommended default: keep the Phase 1 values as labelled placeholders until answered). (2) Same open question from 3.1: which PRD stage `sac_review` is — its 20-day count is honest about the state either way.
- Next suggested task: Phase **3.4** — `escalation_chain.json` + `escalation.py`: L0–L3 at 70/90/100/125%, unassigned-after-1-day rules.

### IMPL-0022 — Phase 3.2: FR-28 return/revert reason code + free text, both mandatory
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-26 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / afe9916 (work uncommitted at implementation)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 3 **3.2** (AGENT-02 workflow hardening); PRD **FR-28** (P0, PS A, line 206: "Return/revert to prior stage or institution; reason code + free text mandatory"); §11 metrics (line 297: "rejections and returns by reason code" — the reason codes are persisted so that metric is computed from structured data, never from free-text guesswork).
- Goal: returns and reverts carry a structured reason code from configuration alongside the (already mandatory) free text, persisted per transition for aggregation and audit.
- What existed before: free text only — `requires_reason` (route config) gated the text on returns (`workflow_service.py:227`) and revert always demanded text (`:549`); `WorkflowTransition` had `reason` but no code; no vocabulary existed anywhere; §11's "by reason code" metric had nothing structured to read.
- What I did (tests first):
  - **Tests** — `backend/tests/test_reason_codes.py` (**21 tests**). Config contract (6): loads `unverified`+`placeholder`; cites FR-28 + the §11 metric; every code has unique id/label/`applies_to`/source; both action kinds have a valid code; **cross-config**: every action in `required_for_actions` exists in `workflow_routes.json` with `requires_reason` true and appears on some route. Behaviour (9): return without code → refused + audited; unknown code → refused, message lists the valid codes (the refusal teaches the vocabulary); code without free text → still refused (both halves mandatory); valid code+text → accepted, stored on the transition row, the audit event carries the code in `after`; a non-return action (`submit`) needs no code; **`reject` needs free text but no code** (FR-28 mandates codes on returns/reverts only — the scoping decision, pinned by test); revert without code → refused + audited; revert with valid code → stored on the undo row; a revert-only code cannot satisfy a return. API (6): `GET /workflow/reason-codes` serves the vocabulary (401 without token); the transition API accepts and refuses codes; `available_transitions` exposes `requires_reason_code` so the review UI can see which actions will demand a code.
  - **Config** — new `backend/rules/reason_codes.json`: 4 clearly-labelled **placeholder** codes (`RC-DOC-MISSING`, `RC-CLARIFICATION`, `RC-SCOPE-ERROR` revert-only, `RC-OTHER` catch-all) each with `applies_to` + per-code source; `required_for_actions: ["return_for_clarification"]` scopes the mandate to config, so adding/removing a return type is a config edit. **The PRD never enumerates codes and §19 has no vocabulary question** → placeholder, the same pattern as 3.3's placeholder day counts; an officer cannot invent a code at the keyboard and this repo does not invent a clause at the desk.
  - **Loader** — `policy.reason_codes_policy()` (`@lru_cache`, same as every other rules file).
  - **Model + migration** — `WorkflowTransition.reason_code` (nullable `String(64)`; nullable because non-return actions carry no code) + `0012_workflow_reason_code.py` (head; `alembic check` clean).
  - **Service** — `workflow_service._reason_code_error(applies_to, code)` returns refusal reasons listing valid codes; `request_transition`/`revert_last_transition` gain `reason_code: str | None`; the mandate is scoped by `reason_codes.required_for_actions` (**not** by `requires_reason` — `reject` also demands free text but the PRD asks no code for it); a supplied-but-unvalidated code is refused even on non-mandated actions (an unknown code must never reach the row and poison the §11 metric); the code is stored on the transition row and echoed in the audit event's `after` dict (transition and revert).
  - **API** — `TransitionRequest.reason_code` / `RevertRequest.reason_code` (optional at schema level so non-return actions stay simple; the service refuses a return that omits/fakes it); pass-throughs; new `GET /workflow/reason-codes` (`get_access`-only, mirroring `/workflow/routes` — no permission closure, so no MATRIX row is needed, per the matrix test's `require`/`require_any` discovery); `available_transitions` entries now carry `requires_reason_code`.
  - **Existing callers updated** — 7 test files that perform returns/reverts now pass valid codes (`test_routes_and_revert`, `test_case_assignment`, `test_concurrency`, `test_deficiency_loop`, `test_ai_copilot`, `test_stage_graph`, `test_transaction_boundary`). Two needed care beyond a mechanical edit: in `test_transaction_boundary.test_audit_write_failure_fails_the_revert...` the code was added deliberately — without it the revert would be refused at the code check and the test would silently stop exercising the broken-audit-store path it exists for; and `test_case_assignment.test_handing_over_twice...` had to be fixed after the full run: it never asserted acceptance, so its codeless return was silently refused, its resubmit no-op'd, and its "no duplicate assignments" assertion had become vacuous (it passed either way).
- Files changed: `backend/rules/reason_codes.json` (new), `backend/app/services/policy.py`, `backend/app/models/workflow.py`, `backend/migrations/versions/0012_workflow_reason_code.py` (new), `backend/app/services/workflow_service.py`, `backend/app/schemas/api.py`, `backend/app/api/workflow.py`, `backend/tests/test_reason_codes.py` (new, 21), + 6 existing test files updated with codes, `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (3.2 ticked), `README.md` + `CONTEXT.md`, this log.
- Migrations / API / config / rule-file changes: **1 column added** (`workflow_transitions.reason_code`, migration 0012; `alembic check` clean); **1 new endpoint** (`GET /workflow/reason-codes`, read-only, signed-in); **1 new rule file** (`reason_codes.json`); `available_transitions` entries gain `requires_reason_code` (additive, no existing field changed). No UI this task (AGENT-11 adopts the vocabulary endpoint separately).
- **Permission and audit effects:** no new permissions and no new gates beyond FR-28's own mandate — the code requirement applies exactly to the returns/reverts that already required a free-text reason (or, for revert, already required one); refusals are audited through the existing `_reject` path (`workflow.*` rejected events); accepted transitions and reverts carry `reason_code` in the audit `after` dict so the §11 "by reason code" metric reads structured data end to end.
- Decisions made: (1) **The vocabulary ships as a labelled placeholder** — the PRD mandates a code but never enumerates codes and §19 has no vocabulary question; same handling as 3.3's day counts (AGENTS.md "when requirements are ambiguous"). (2) **Mandate scoped by `required_for_actions` config, not `requires_reason`** — `reject` also demands free text but FR-28 asks a code only for return/revert; keying on the route flag would have silently extended the mandate to rejections. (3) **Codes are validated even when not required** — an unvalidated code must never reach the row (metric poisoning). (4) **Scope-suffixed vocabulary** — `applies_to` prevents a revert-only code from being used to return a case. (5) **Code rides in the audit `after`, not `reason`** — the free-text reason field stays prose; the metric reads `after.reason_code`. (6) **No new DEC entry** — follows DEC-0003 (config-driven, no new gates) and the placeholder rule already logged with 2.4's lease duration.
- Verification: exact commands + results:
  - `pytest tests/test_reason_codes.py -q -p no:randomly` → **21 passed** (after one fix: a "return-only code" test was rewritten as "revert-only code on a return" — the shipped vocabulary has no return-only code).
  - First full run → **13 failed / 429 passed**: every failure an existing return/revert caller without a code (expected — the mandate landed); all fixed by adding valid codes. One near-miss caught by reading, not by failure: `test_audit_write_failure_fails_the_revert` would have *passed* while silently testing the wrong path (code refusal instead of audit failure) — code added so it keeps exercising its intended guarantee.
  - `pytest -q -p no:randomly` → **442 passed, 1 warning in 42.89s** (0 skipped; +21 vs 421)
  - `make lint` → `All checks passed!`
  - `SIH_DATABASE_URL=sqlite:////tmp/sih32.db alembic upgrade head` → `0011_stage_instances -> 0012_workflow_reason_code` applied; `alembic check` → `No new upgrade operations detected.`
  - Not run: `npm run build` (no frontend change), Compose/Postgres (SQLite only).
- Status per requirement touched: Plan 3.2 "Return/revert with reason code + free text (FR-28); audit" — **Implemented · Tested**. Return and revert both refuse to proceed without a valid code + free text; the code is persisted on the transition row and the audit event.
- Limitations / assumptions / manual steps:
  - **The vocabulary is a placeholder** (4 codes); AICTE must confirm the official reason-code list before the codes are treated as anything but Unverified. Changing the list is a config edit + human review, not a code change.
  - `reject` carries free text but no code (PRD asks codes for return/revert only); §11's "rejections by reason code" metric therefore has no structured input for rejections yet — flagged as an open question below.
  - No UI: officers cannot yet pick a code from a dropdown (AGENT-11); the API refuses codeless returns meanwhile, so the demo path is API-driven for this feature.
  - Existing transition rows keep `reason_code` NULL (nullable column; historical returns predate the mandate).
  - Not verified against Postgres/Compose; SQLite only.
- Related bugs: none logged as BUG-#### (no failures beyond the expected mandate fallout; nothing regressed).
- Open questions for a human: (1) **The official FR-28 reason-code vocabulary** — which codes does AICTE use for returns/reverts? Recommended default (implemented): the 4-code labelled placeholder, `RC-OTHER` as catch-all. (2) **Should rejections also carry a code?** §11 asks for "rejections … by reason code" but FR-28 mandates codes only on return/revert — recommended default: keep as-is until AICTE answers, then extend `required_for_actions` (config change). (3) When the UI adopts this, should `RC-OTHER` free text be reviewable (an officer selecting "other" with no description is nearly a codeless return) — a UI-level guard, noted for AGENT-11.
- Next suggested task: Phase **3.3** — `stage_limits.json` with placeholder day counts, clearly labelled; per-stage due dates.

### IMPL-0021 — Phase 3.1: FR-09 stage graph — derived stage rows that follow authorised moves
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-26 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / 2a758ed (work uncommitted at implementation)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 3 **3.1** (AGENT-02 workflow hardening); PRD **FR-09** (P0, PS A) "Auto-advance when authorised user marks stage complete"; the §7 stage table (lines 118–127: Readiness, Routing, Scrutiny/Re-Scrutiny, Compliance cycle, EVC, SHC, Executive Committee, Appeal, Grant); line 20 (prototype workflow states are the **baseline subset** the PRD stages extend).
- Goal: give every case a live, derived record of where it sits in the PRD §7 stage list, advanced automatically by the transitions and reverts the workflow service already authorises — with the stage service holding no authority of its own.
- What existed before: `workflow_service.request_transition`/`revert_last_transition` moved `current_state` through the four config routes (`workflow_routes.json`) and committed once each; `auto_transition_if_available` moved cases on operational triggers; **no stage concept existed anywhere** — a case was only a state, so nothing could answer "which PRD stage is this case in, and since when" (the §7/FR-09 gap).
- What I did (tests first):
  - **Tests** — `backend/tests/test_stage_graph.py` (**18 tests**). Config contract (7): loads `unverified`+`advisory_only`; cites FR-09 + §7 and starts with `md/1_PRD.md`; **every route lists all eight PRD stage keys in lifecycle order** (`readiness…grant`, no renamed/omitted stages); every stage cites a source and stages with empty `active_states` carry a note; **`active_states` may only name states that route's own transitions define** (cross-config against `workflow_routes.json` — the "no invented states" guard); one stage owns each state within a route (no state means two stages active); status vocabulary is exactly `pending|active|completed`. Behaviour (10): submit opens readiness only; an authorised completion auto-advances with `completed_by` set; return opens compliance_cycle; **re-entry keeps one row per stage and reactivates it** (Re-Scrutiny ≠ second row); a terminal decision completes only entered stages (EVC/Appeal/Grant stay pending — "approved" never reads as "appeal completed"); a refused transition changes no stage row; a revert rewinds the graph with the case; sync never touches `baseline_version`/`current_state`/transition rows; sync is idempotent; unmapped `draft` stays pending; the JSON file exists.
  - **Config** — new `backend/rules/stage_graph.json`: per-route ordered 8-stage lists with `active_states` (new_technical: readiness[submitted], scrutiny[scrutiny], compliance_cycle[returned], evc[evc_scheduled+evc_completed]; existing_technical adds no compliance/evc mapping — that route defines no such states; evc_only maps compliance+evc but no scrutiny; evc_sac_shc adds `sac_review`), `terminal_states: [approved, rejected]` (mirrors `TERMINAL_STATES`, cross-tested), statuses list, and a note per unreachable stage. **`sac_review` is deliberately mapped to no stage**: the PRD §7 order does not say whether it is SHC or Executive Committee, so a case there shows no active stage rather than a mislabelled one (open question below).
  - **Model** — `StageInstance` (`stage_instances` table, in `models/workflow.py`): unique application+stage_key, `sequence` (config order), `status`, `started_at`/`completed_at`/`completed_by`; one row per stage per case, re-entry reactivates. Exported from `models/__init__.py`. Docstring pins the derived/no-authority contract.
  - **Migration** — `0011_stage_instances.py` (hand-written, 0010 style; head was `0010_readiness_flags`).
  - **Service** — `app/services/stages.py`: `stage_graph_policy()` (KeyError if a route is missing — a config hole must fail loudly, not derive nothing), `statuses()`, and `sync_stage_instances(db, application, *, actor_id, from_state, retract)`. Derivation: the stage owning `current_state` activates (idempotent — an active row keeps its stint clock); a forward move completes the stints it left; with `retract=True` (undo only) the pulled-back stage's stint is retracted to pending, not completed. Terminal states complete the decision stage + active stints + **"provably entered" earlier stages**: a pending earlier stage completes only when blocking all its states disconnects the decision state from `draft` in that route's own transition graph (so a seeded case born mid-flow gets readiness completed honestly, while compliance_cycle off the direct EVC path stays pending). Backfilled rows keep `started_at` NULL — no guessed start times.
  - **Hooks** — `workflow_service.request_transition` (accepted path, immediately before its `db.commit()`) and `revert_last_transition` (same, with `retract=True`) call the sync **in the same transaction**; refused transitions return earlier and change nothing; `_reject` paths never call it.
- Files changed: `backend/rules/stage_graph.json` (new), `backend/app/models/workflow.py`, `backend/app/models/__init__.py`, `backend/migrations/versions/0011_stage_instances.py` (new), `backend/app/services/stages.py` (new), `backend/app/services/policy.py`, `backend/app/services/workflow_service.py`, `backend/tests/test_stage_graph.py` (new, 18), `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (3.1 ticked), `README.md` + `CONTEXT.md`, this log.
- Migrations / API / config / rule-file changes: **1 new table** (`stage_instances`, migration 0011; `alembic check` clean); **no new endpoint** (the stage rows are readable through the existing case APIs in later tasks; no UI this task); **1 new rule file** (`stage_graph.json`). No existing endpoint, permission, transition or deadline behaviour changed — transitions behave exactly as before, they now also maintain stage rows.
- **Permission and audit effects:** none new — the sync runs only for transitions/reverts that were already permission-checked and audited (`workflow.*` events unchanged); the stage rows add no audit events of their own (the transition event is the record; duplicating it would double-count state changes), and no transition reads the rows, so no gate is introduced (DEC-0003).
- Decisions made: (1) **Derived rows, never stored verdicts** — rows re-derive from config + current state on every move, so a stage-graph config edit cannot strand stale rows (same principle as 2.3's un-stored evaluations). (2) **One row per stage, re-entry reactivates** — Re-Scrutiny is a second stint on the scrutiny row; stint history lives in audit/workflow_transitions, not duplicated here. (3) **Forward vs undo** — a forward move *completes* the stint it left (including the compliance cycle when resubmit ends it); an undo *retracts* it to pending (nothing completed it); the caller passes `retract=True` only from `revert_last_transition`. (4) **Terminal decisions complete only entered stages**, with a cut-set proof for stages entered before rows existed (seeded cases) — an unentered stage is never marked completed. (5) **`sac_review` maps to no stage** — the PRD does not say which stage it is; under-reporting with a note beats mislabelling a regulatory stage (Master Prompt §2 rule 4). (6) **No API yet** — FR-09's "auto-advance" is the mechanism; surfacing stage rows to screens is AGENT-11. (7) No new DEC entry — follows DEC-0003 (derived/advisory bookkeeping, no gating).
- Verification: exact commands + results:
  - First `pytest tests/test_stage_graph.py -q -p no:randomly` → **3 failed / 15 passed**: (a) freshly created rows read `status = None` in memory before flush (the column default applies at INSERT), so every `== "active"` / `!= "pending"` comparison silently skipped them — readiness/scrutiny never completed; root cause found by reproducing the sync in a scratch session and printing row statuses; fix: create rows with explicit `status="pending"`. (b) The same run exposed the forward-vs-undo design gap: `resubmit` (a forward transition landing back at readiness) was retracting the compliance cycle to pending instead of completing its stint — fixed with the explicit `retract` flag (only `revert_last_transition` passes True); `_activate` also made idempotent (an active row keeps its stint clock on re-sync).
  - `pytest tests/test_stage_graph.py -q -p no:randomly` → **18 passed**.
  - `make lint` → `All checks passed!`
  - `make test` → **421 passed, 1 warning in 42.96s** (0 skipped; +18 vs 403)
  - `SIH_DATABASE_URL=sqlite:////tmp/sih31.db alembic upgrade head` → `0010_readiness_flags -> 0011_stage_instances` applied; `alembic check` → `No new upgrade operations detected.`
  - Not run: `npm run build` (no frontend change), Compose/Postgres (SQLite only).
- Status per requirement touched: Plan 3.1 "`StageInstance` model + migration; `stages.py` auto-advance on authorised completion (FR-09)" — **Implemented · Tested**. Auto-advance happens on every authorised completion and revert, in the same transaction, derived from config; the stage rows exist per case from submission onward.
- Limitations / assumptions / manual steps:
  - **`sac_review` has no active stage** (open mapping question above): a case in that state shows all stages pending/completed-past with none active. Escalation/due-date work in 3.3/3.4 must not assume an active stage exists for every non-terminal state.
  - Stage rows are created lazily — a case created directly mid-flow by the seed gets its rows on the first transition or sync; until then it has none (the seeded demo cases have no rows until moved).
  - Backfilled completions carry `completed_at` = the sync moment and `completed_by` = the deciding actor, with `started_at` NULL — the true entry time is not recorded anywhere and is not guessed.
  - Re-Scrutiny resets the scrutiny row's stint clock; earlier stints are only in the audit timeline, not on the row.
  - No UI — the rows are backend state (AGENT-11 renders them).
  - Not verified against Postgres/Compose; SQLite only.
- Related bugs: none logged as BUG-#### (both failures were caught and fixed within the task before the final green run; no production behaviour regressed — the row-status one is recorded here with root cause, per the log's own standard).
- Open questions for a human: (1) Which PRD stage is the prototype's **`sac_review` state** — SHC, Executive Committee, or a prototype-only step? Recommended default (implemented): map to none until confirmed; do not guess a regulatory label. (2) Should stage rows be exposed via an API this phase, or is the workflow/case API enough until the review UI (AGENT-11)? (3) When SHC/Appeal/Grant states are eventually added, that is a new regulatory route — needs human approval, not a config edit (config note says so).
- Next suggested task: Phase **3.2** — return/revert with reason code + free text (FR-28); audit.

### IMPL-0020 — Phase 2.5: FR-01 stubbed portal feed — the seven fields, with an "as of" stamp
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-26 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / dfdad18 (work uncommitted at implementation)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 2 **2.5** (AGENT-08 intake); PRD **FR-01** (P0, APH cl. **2.3.1**) "Receive each final-submitted application with ID, PID, category, institution type, submission time, TER status, documents"; §18 risk row "Portal data not real-time | Scheduled sync with 'as of' time on every screen"; release plan "Prototype (sample data, stubbed portal feed)".
- Goal: make the portal-feed interface real — the exact FR-01 field set, served from recorded case data, every response stamped with `as_of` and labelled a stub — so clients can be built against it and no one can mistake it for live portal data.
- What existed before: **nothing portal-related** anywhere in the backend (repo-wide search for `portal|pid|ter_status` found only unrelated matches — DB roles, TERMINAL_STATES, "REGISTERED LEASE DEED"); no PID column, no institution-type column (institutions carry name/AICTE-id/region only), no TER payment record anywhere; `applications.submitted_at` exists and the submit transition sets it; `authorization_service.scope_filter` is the established list-scope predicate; `policy.py` is the established rule-file loader.
- What I did (tests first):
  - **Tests** — `backend/tests/test_portal_feed.py` (**17 tests**). First run failed at collection (`ModuleNotFoundError: app.services.portal_feed`), proving the module was absent. Config contract: loads `unverified`+`advisory_only`; cites FR-01/cl. 2.3.1/§18 "as of" row and starts with `md/1_PRD.md`; **declares exactly the seven FR-01 fields in the PRD's own order**; `pid`/`institution_type`/`ter_status` are `modelled: false` with descriptions while the four recorded fields are `modelled: true`; `sync_cadence` is **null** with a "not recorded/placeholder" note (no cadence invented); the note admits it is a **stub** and names the ingestion gap. Service (with a global-scope stand-in access object): envelope carries `source: "stub"` + `as_of` = the fixed clock (2026-10-22T09:00:00) + `status`/`advisory_only` from config; **only final-submitted cases appear** (`submitted_at` set; the draft `app-draft-0004` never does); every record has exactly the seven keys **in order**; the three unmodelled fields are **null, never guessed** (`ter_status` never a claimed "paid"); modelled fields carry recorded values (category, ISO submission time, documents with `doc_type`/`file_name`/`version`/`status` — `lease_deed` present for the main case); records are in deterministic (sorted-id) order; the feed writes nothing (`baseline_version` unchanged). Endpoints: stub + `as_of` served; **scope enforced** — the seeded scrutiny officer (scope `assigned`) and the demo institution each see exactly their own cases and `app-second-0002` (a different institution) never leaks; a Regional officer sees a strict subset; unauthenticated → **401**. Matrix row added (`GET /api/v1/portal/feed`: `READ_ANY`).
  - **Config** — new `backend/rules/portal_feed.json`: identity/citation (FR-01, cl. 2.3.1, §18 "as of" row, release-plan stub line), `status: unverified`, `advisory_only`, `note` (explicitly a STUB; the **ingestion half is deliberately not implemented** — external integration + role-authority change = human gates), `as_of_note` (clock-derived, "valid as of that instant and no later"), `sync_cadence: null` + note (PRD says "scheduled sync" but records no cadence — placeholder, like the empty `calendars.json` years), `fields_note` + `submitted_note`, and the seven `fields` entries with `modelled` flags and per-field descriptions of what a live feed would carry.
  - **Loader** — `policy.py` gains `portal_feed_policy()` (lru_cached `_load`, the established pattern).
  - **Service** — new `app/services/portal_feed.py`: `portal_feed_policy()` (copy of config), `_record()` (the seven keys in the PRD's order; unmodelled → `None`; documents embedded as briefs sorted by type+version), and `build_feed(db, access)` (submitted-only query ordered by id, `scope_filter` applied exactly as the applications list does, documents fetched in one batch excluding rejected, envelope `{source, as_of, status, advisory_only, sync_cadence, records}`). Read-only: no writes, no audit, no state change.
  - **API** — new `api/portal.py`: `GET /api/v1/portal/feed` gated by `require_any(APPLICATION_READ, CASE_READ)`; schemas `PortalFeedOut`/`PortalFeedRecord`/`PortalDocumentBrief` in `schemas/api.py`; router registered in `main.py` (import + router tuple).
- Files changed: `backend/rules/portal_feed.json` (new), `backend/app/services/policy.py`, `backend/app/services/portal_feed.py` (new), `backend/app/api/portal.py` (new), `backend/app/schemas/api.py`, `backend/app/main.py`, `backend/tests/test_portal_feed.py` (new, 17), `backend/tests/test_authz_matrix.py` (1 MATRIX row), `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (2.5 ticked), `README.md` + `CONTEXT.md`, this log.
- Migrations / API / config / rule-file changes: **no model change, no migration** (the feed is computed from existing tables; PID/institution-type/TER-status are served as null rather than columns being invented for a stub); **1 new endpoint** (GET only); **1 new rule file** (`portal_feed.json`). No existing endpoint, permission, transition or deadline behaviour changed.
- **Permission and audit effects:** none new — `READ_ANY` (`APPLICATION_READ|CASE_READ`) plus the existing `scope_filter`; no permission constant added, no role change, no audit events (a read writes nothing). The feed never bypasses scope: the endpoint test proves an other-institution case does not leak to the demo institution or the assigned-only scrutiny officer.
- Decisions made: (1) **Outbound GET stub, not inbound ingestion** — FR-01's verb is "receive", but an inbound writing endpoint would need a portal service identity (new role authority) and an untrusted-write path (external integration); both are human approval gates in AGENTS.md, so the prototype serves the field set and records ingestion as an open question. (2) **Unmodelled fields are `null`, never guessed** — no PID/institution-type/TER-status data exists in the repo, and a stub that invented them would poison anything built against it (same discipline as 2.3's placeholders and 2.4's null duration). (3) **`sync_cadence` ships null** — the PRD records no cadence, so none is invented (labelled placeholder, AGENTS.md). (4) **`as_of` from the injectable clock**, echoed in the payload per §18 — never `datetime.now()`, never a hidden freshness claim. (5) **Scope-filtered with the same `scope_filter` as the applications list** — a feed is not a licence to widen visibility; rejected documents are excluded (the document API serves content under its own permission). (6) No new DEC entry — follows DEC-0003 (stub/advisory) and the untrusted-ingestion rule.
- Verification: exact commands + results:
  - `pytest tests/test_portal_feed.py -q -p no:randomly` → first run **collection error** (`ModuleNotFoundError`) — module genuinely absent.
  - `pytest tests/test_portal_feed.py tests/test_authz_matrix.py -q -p no:randomly` → **2 failed / 36 passed**: my two endpoint tests assumed broader seeded scopes than exist (scrutiny is `assigned`-scoped, not global; the demo institution owns two cases, not one) — **tests corrected to the real seeded facts** (which is itself the scope-leak proof), then **38 passed**.
  - `make lint` → `All checks passed!`
  - `make test` → **403 passed, 1 warning in 37.82s** (0 skipped; +17 vs 386)
  - `python3 -c "json.load(...)"` on `portal_feed.json` → valid.
  - Not run: `alembic check` (no model change), `npm run build` (no frontend change), Compose/Postgres (SQLite only).
- Status per requirement touched: Plan 2.5 "Stubbed portal feed endpoint carrying the FR-01 fields, with 'as of' timestamp" — **Implemented · Tested**. All seven FR-01 fields are carried (four from recorded data, three as declared nulls), every response carries `as_of` and `source: "stub"`, scope is enforced, nothing is written. The "receive" (ingestion) half is deliberately unbuilt — see limitations/open questions.
- Limitations / assumptions / manual steps:
  - **Ingestion is not implemented** — no external record can create or modify a case here; the feed is outbound-only. Building it needs a human decision on the portal service identity and the trust model (open question 1).
  - PID, institution type and TER status are `null` for every record until those sources are integrated; the config names each gap.
  - `sync_cadence` is null — no schedule is asserted anywhere.
  - "Final-submitted" = `submitted_at` recorded (config `submitted_note`); drafts never appear.
  - No UI — the feed is an API (frontend work is AGENT-11).
  - Not verified against Postgres/Compose; SQLite only.
- Related bugs: none logged (the 2 test failures were my own wrong assumptions about seeded scopes, fixed in the test file before the final green run; no production behaviour was involved).
- Open questions for a human: (1) Should the **inbound** half of FR-01 be built (portal → system ingestion)? Needs: a portal service identity (role authority), the trust/auth model for the channel, and a dedup/idempotency contract — all approval gates. Recommended default (implemented): outbound stub only. (2) Integrate PID, institution type and TER payment status sources so the three nulls can carry real values — owner: product/integration. (3) Record the official **sync cadence** in `portal_feed.json` once AICTE specifies it (§18 says "scheduled sync" without a period).
- Next suggested task: the **Phase 2 exit check** (late app → next-AY queue; blocked flags visible to Regional officer — both already covered by `test_readiness_cutoff`/`test_blocked_flags` and needing only verification), then Phase 3.

### IMPL-0019 — Phase 2.4: FR-05 lease-years computation — remaining lease vs longest course duration
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-26 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / 7c192f5 (work uncommitted at implementation)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 2 **2.4** (AGENT-08 intake/readiness); PRD **FR-05** (P0) "Compute remaining lease years vs longest course duration; flag" (APH cl. **2.1e**), **FR-45** (P0) "Read lease end date, compute years, finding if equal or shorter; store end date for expiry alerts", the cl. 2.1e note (line 53: "EoA stops once the live lease equals the longest course duration unless extended by 30 years. Compute and flag remaining years during verification"), and `RULE-LEASE-01` in `compliance_rules.json` ("Remaining lease years > longest course duration, or 30-yr extension on file | 2.1e | Deficiency candidate").
- Goal: compute and surface the remaining-lease-years comparison per case as advisory configuration-driven output, reusing the grounded lease extractor's persisted evidence — with no invented duration, no gating, and an honest `needs_review` where the data is not recorded.
- What existed before: `grounded_lease_extractor.py` + `ocr_service.DispatchingExtractor` extract `valid_to` at upload for `doc_type == "lease_agreement"` (grounded: value + verbatim source_quote + reasoning; normalised to ISO by `_to_iso_date`); `evidence_service.build_snapshot`/`latest_document_fields` already expose per-doc-type persisted fields + a `verified` map; `compliance_rules.json` has `LEASE_VALID_COVERS_PERIOD`/`LEASE_DATE_CONSISTENCY` (engine does **no** year arithmetic and gates nothing per DEC-0003); `validation_service` drafts deficiencies for unparseable/past expiry (a separate concern); `reporting_service`/`monitoring_service` band expiry dates for alerts. **No** longest-course-duration figure exists anywhere in the repo; nothing computed remaining lease years vs a course duration.
- What I did (tests first):
  - **Tests** — `backend/tests/test_lease_check.py` (**23 tests**). Config contract: block loads `unverified`+`advisory_only`; cites FR-05/FR-45/2.1e/RULE-LEASE-01 and starts with `md/1_PRD.md`; **`longest_course_duration_years` is null** with a note naming §19 Q11 (nothing invented); `extension_years == 30` labelled Unverified; `extension_on_file` declared `modelled: false`; evidence note pins "persisted extractor output / never re-parse". Maths: remaining years from the fixed clock (2026-10-22 → 2028-03-31 ≈ 1.44); `anniversary()` clamps 29 Feb → 28 Feb (and keeps 29 Feb in leap years); the comparison uses that clamped anniversary. Three-valued evaluation: no lease → `needs_review` (never a pass); **shipped null duration → `needs_review` with years still computed**; shorter → `insufficient`; exceeds → `sufficient`; **equality = "stopped unless extended"** (`None` → `needs_review`, `False` → `insufficient`, `True` → `sufficient`); expired lease → `insufficient` **without** a duration (a proof); unverified expiry never yields `sufficient` (and demotes decisive answers to `needs_review` with the reason disclosed); vocabulary guard (no pass/fail/rejected/blocked); purity (repeat calls equal, `baseline_version` unchanged). Endpoints: Regional officer sees computed years + honest `needs_review` on the seeded verified lease; lease-less case → `needs_review` with `valid_to: null`; **uploaded `lease_agreement` PDF → check reads the grounded extractor's persisted ISO date (2031-06-30) with `verified: false` and "not verified" in the reason** — no re-parse at read time; institution may read; out-of-scope case → 404. Matrix row added (`GET .../lease-check`: `READ_ANY`).
  - **Config** — `readiness_policy.json` gains a `lease` block: identity (`RULE-LEASE-01`, clause `2.1e`, source citing FR-05/FR-45/line-53 note/compliance rule), `advisory_only` note (stores nothing; FR-45's "store end date" is already satisfied by the extracted field + alerting services, so no duplicate store), **`longest_course_duration_years: null`** + `duration_note` (§19 Q11 open; never a guessed verdict), `extension_years: 30` + Unverified note, `extension_on_file` `modelled: false` (absent register ⇒ equal-duration is unknown, never a silent pass), `evidence_note` (snapshot-only, deterministic, unverified disclosed), `anniversary_note` (29 Feb → 28 Feb documented).
  - **Service** — `readiness.py` gains `lease_policy()` (KeyError if block deleted), `anniversary()` (29 Feb clamp), `remaining_years()` (days/365.2425, 2 dp — display only; comparisons are exact anniversary dates), `_unwrap()`/`_parse_date()` (handles bare ISO seeded values *and* the extractor's `{value, source_quote, ai_reasoning}` dicts; unparseable → `None`, never a guessed date), `lease_evidence(db, application)` (reads `evidence_service.build_snapshot`, prefers `lease_deed` then `lease_agreement`, returns `(valid_to, verified)` via `snapshot.is_verified`), and `lease_check(valid_to, *, cfg=None, as_of=None, extension_on_file=None, verified=True)` returning identity + inputs + `remaining_years` + `comparison` (`exceeds|equals|shorter|not_comparable`) + three-valued `evaluation` (`sufficient|insufficient|needs_review`) + human `reason` + `missing_inputs`.
  - **API** — `api/readiness.py` router prefix widened to `/applications/{application_id}` (paths now `/blocked-flags`, `/blocked-flags/{flag_id}`, `/lease-check`; existing URLs and matrix rows unchanged) + `GET /applications/{id}/lease-check` (`require_any(APPLICATION_READ, CASE_READ)`, scope → 404) calling `lease_evidence` then `lease_check` with the clock's business date. Read-only: no writes, no audit (nothing changes), no request body. Schema `LeaseCheckOut` in `schemas/api.py`.
- Files changed: `backend/rules/readiness_policy.json`, `backend/app/services/readiness.py`, `backend/app/api/readiness.py`, `backend/app/schemas/api.py`, `backend/tests/test_lease_check.py` (new, 23), `backend/tests/test_authz_matrix.py` (1 MATRIX row), `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (2.4 ticked), `README.md` + `CONTEXT.md` (counts/suite list/API map), this log.
- Migrations / API / config / rule-file changes: **no model change** (nothing new is stored — the check is computed on read, like every clock here), so no migration; **1 new endpoint** (GET only); new `lease` block in an existing rule file. No existing endpoint, permission, transition or deadline behaviour changed.
- **Permission and audit effects:** none new — GET uses the same `READ_ANY` the blocked-flags GET uses; no permission constant added, no role change, no audit events (a read writes nothing by design).
- Decisions made: (1) **Duration ships null** — no figure is recorded anywhere and PRD §19 Q11 is open, so the comparison answers `needs_review` while the remaining-years computation still runs (AGENTS.md: labelled placeholder, move on; Master Prompt §2 rule 4). (2) **Equality = stopped unless extended** — implemented exactly as the clause words it; only a *recorded* extension flips it, and "no extension record" is unknown, not false (consistent with 2.3's absent-data rule). (3) **Expired lease is `insufficient` without a duration** — a deduction from recorded dates, not a guess; it is the only duration-free decisive answer. (4) **Unverified evidence never yields a decisive answer** — mirrors the rule engine's `unverified_outcome` discipline; the `verified` flag rides in the response. (5) **Reuses the persisted extractor output** via `build_snapshot` — never re-parses PDFs, never calls a model at read time (deterministic; the external-call boundary stays at upload, where it is already gated and mocked). (6) **No new storage for the end date** — FR-45's "store end date for expiry alerts" is already satisfied by the extracted field plus `reporting_service`/`monitoring_service` alert bands; duplicating it would create a second source of truth. (7) Both `lease_deed` (seeded/rules) and `lease_agreement` (the only extractor-dispatched type) are consulted, deed preferred. (8) No new DEC entry — follows DEC-0003 (advisory) and the Unverified-rules-never-gate rule.
- Verification: exact commands + results:
  - `pytest tests/test_lease_check.py tests/test_authz_matrix.py -q -p no:randomly` → first run **1 failed / 43 passed**: the uploaded-lease path returned `needs_review` for the null duration *before* the unverified-evidence disclosure, so "not verified" never appeared in the reason (test was right; disclosure must be on every path) → service fixed → **44 passed**. (Preceding run: `NameError: name 'require' is not defined` — I removed the `require` import while editing the router prefix but the POST route still uses it → import restored.)
  - `make lint` → `All checks passed!`
  - `make test` → **386 passed, 1 warning in 35.26s** (0 skipped; +23 vs 363)
  - `python3 -c "json.load(...)"` on `readiness_policy.json` → valid.
  - Not run: `alembic check` (no model/migration change this task), `npm run build` (no frontend change), Compose/Postgres (SQLite only).
- Status per requirement touched: Plan 2.4 "Lease-years computation and flag (FR-05, RULE-LEASE-01)" — **Implemented · Tested** for the computation + flag-shaped advisory output (remaining years, comparison, three-valued evaluation, reason, missing inputs, evidence provenance). The durable "flag" half (an officer clear/hold row + a deficiency candidate record) is deliberately *not* duplicated here: RULE-LEASE-01's "Deficiency candidate" wording maps onto the existing structured deficiency loop (Phase 1.6) which an officer already drives, and 2.3's flag machinery exists for the FR-03 rule set; wiring lease findings into those is a UI/integration step (AGENT-11) rather than new regulatory behaviour.
- Limitations / assumptions / manual steps:
  - **The comparison can never be decisive in production until a human records `longest_course_duration_years`** in `readiness_policy.json` (per institution today — the config is global; per-institution durations need a data source this repo lacks). Every live lease therefore answers `needs_review` with real computed years. This is the honest state, surfaced in `reason` + `missing_inputs`.
  - `extension_on_file` is unmodelled: an equal-duration lease answers `needs_review` rather than assuming "no extension".
  - Feb-29 anniversaries clamp to Feb-28 (documented assumption, config note + test).
  - `remaining_years` uses 365.2425 days/year (display only; comparisons are exact anniversary dates).
  - No UI — visible via API (review UI is AGENT-11, as with 2.3).
  - Not verified against Postgres/Compose; SQLite only.
- Related bugs: none logged (the two failures above were my own import removal and an incomplete disclosure path, both caught and fixed within the task before the final green run; no production behaviour regressed).
- Open questions for a human: (1) Record the institution's **longest course duration** (PRD §19 Q11 / cl. 2.1e) so the comparison can become decisive — owner: regulatory/product. (2) Should "no 30-year extension record on file" eventually be treated as "not extended" (today: unknown → `needs_review`)? Recommended default: keep unknown until an extension register exists. (3) Should lease findings auto-draft a `RULE-LEASE-01` deficiency (the validation service already drafts expiry deficiencies for `lease_agreement` uploads) — owner: product.
- Next suggested task: Phase 2 **2.5** — stubbed portal feed endpoint with FR-01 fields + "as of" timestamp.

### IMPL-0018 — Phase 2.3: blocked-application flags — five FR-03 rules, officer clear/hold
- Date / agent / branch / commit: 2026-10-26 / MiMo (OpenCode agent, MiMo-V2.6) / master / 6af18e2 (work uncommitted at implementation)
- Task ID and PRD IDs: Phase 2 **2.3** (AGENT-08 intake/readiness with AGENT-06 officer-action patterns); PRD **FR-03** (P0) "Flag blocked applications: Closure applicants applying for other categories; no EoA for 6 years; standalone PGDM/PGCM without full student data; intake increase during Central/State inquiry; partial approval of programmes" (APH cl. **2.21f, 2.16.1a, 2.3.1, 2.6e, 2.27**); §7 readiness row ("Regional officer clears flags … All pass, or official clears/holds each flag"). Feeds the Phase 2 exit check ("blocked flags visible to Regional officer").
- Goal: put the five FR-03 block rules in configuration, evaluate them honestly per case, and let an officer record a clear/hold decision — with persistence, permission, scope and audit, and without any flag ever blocking, rejecting or guessing.
- What existed before: **no** flags table, no flag evaluation, no readiness router (repo-wide search for `blocked|flagged|flag_id` found only unrelated uses); `readiness_policy.json` had only the 2.2 `cut_off` block; `deficiencies.py` was the established officer-action pattern (gate → `require_application` scope→404 → mutate → `baseline_version += 1` → `AuditService.append` → commit); `test_authz_matrix.py` **fails on purpose** until every new gated route is written into its `MATRIX` (+ a valid `JSON_BODIES` probe so a refusal can't be a 422); seed creates **no** `application_items`, and `test_sequencing.py` sets the precedent that absent items are never read as meaningful false. Permissions: `CASE_TRANSITION` already held by regional/scrutiny/admin.
- What I did (tests first):
  - **Tests** — `backend/tests/test_blocked_flags.py` (**31 tests**). First run failed at collection (`ImportError: cannot import name 'ReadinessFlag'`), proving the model was absent. Coverage: config loads `unverified`+`advisory_only`, cites FR-03 and all five clauses, ships **exactly the PRD's five rule wordings with clauses positionally mapped**, and the positional-mapping caveat is written down (with the 2.16.1a cross-confirmation); every flag cites its own clause and declares inputs + condition consistent with each other; **config contract** — every condition uses only `equals`/`contains`/`gte`; the four "not modelled" flags admit `modelled: false` (honesty pin); decision vocabulary is `{cleared, held}` in config **and** the pydantic schema (`rejected`/`blocked`/`open`/`""` all raise `ValidationError`); a real case (nothing modelled) → **all five `needs_review`, never a guessed clear**; evaluation vocabulary guard (no `rejected`/`blocked`/`pass`/`fail`, `advisory_only`, `decision is None` until an officer acts); **absent items ≠ false** for bf-04; a case with Table 2.3 items that aren't t23-09 → `clear` (recorded input provably fails — the rule cannot apply); a t23-09 case → still `needs_review` (inquiry input not recorded); missing inputs are *named*; three-valued logic via synthetic config: all-recorded-and-matching → `flagged`, one recorded failure → `clear` **even with an unknown elsewhere**, only unknowns → `needs_review`; `contains` and `gte` operators each exercised on all three branches; an unsupported operator **raises `ValueError`** (loud, not a silent wrong reason); evaluation is **pure** (two calls equal, zero `ReadinessFlag` rows written); endpoints — Regional officer GET sees all five (Phase 2 exit check), institution may read but gets **403** on decide, clear persists + GET merges + `baseline_version` bumps + exactly one `readiness_flag.decided` audit with before/after/hash, hold works, re-decision keeps **one row** and audits **both** decisions, unknown flag id → **404**, decision `rejected` → **422**, out-of-scope case → **404** (never 403). Plus: 2 `MATRIX` rows + 1 `JSON_BODIES` probe added to `test_authz_matrix.py` (its coverage test fails until a new gated route is declared deliberately — passed after the rows were written).
  - **Config** — `backend/rules/readiness_policy.json` gains a `blocked_flags` section: `status: unverified`, `advisory_only`, source citing FR-03 + all five clauses + §7; `evaluation_note` (three-valued semantics; `clear` means "rule provably does not apply", **not** an official clearance); `clause_mapping_note` (positional mapping from the PRD's two parallel lists, labelled Unverified, cross-confirmed for bf-02↔2.16.1a by the PRD's clause-notes table and bf-04↔cl. 2.6 by PRD §6 rule 6); `input_note` (field names are **declared placeholders for data not integrated** — labels, not invented data); `decisions: [cleared, held]` with a note that there is deliberately no `rejected`; and the five `flags`, each `{flag_id, rule (PRD's exact wording), clause, source, status, advisory_only, inputs[], condition[]}`. Only bf-04's `application.category_ids contains t23-09` is backed by modelled data (Table 2.3 items); all other inputs are `modelled: false`.
  - **Model** — `ReadinessFlag` (`readiness_flags`) in `models/application.py`: one row per application + `flag_id` (unique constraint) holding only the officer's `decision` + `note` + `decided_by`/`created_at`/`decided_at`; exported from `models/__init__.py`. The **evaluation is never stored** (computed on read) so a rule edit can't leave stale verdicts — same principle as the deadline clocks.
  - **Migration** — `migrations/versions/0010_readiness_flags.py` (hand-written in the 0008/0009 style): table + FKs (CASCADE on application, RESTRICT-ish plain FK on user) + unique constraint + `ix_readiness_flags_application_id`.
  - **Service** — `readiness.py` gains `blocked_flags_policy()` (KeyError if the block is deleted — fail loudly), `decision_vocabulary()` (from config), `_build_context()` (only genuinely recorded fields; `category_ids` **omitted entirely** when a case has no items), `_conjunct_holds()` (three operators; unsupported → `ValueError`), `_evaluate_condition()` (scans **all** conjuncts before deciding so a provable failure isn't masked by an earlier unknown → `True`/`False`/`None` + reason), and `evaluate_blocked_flags(application, *, cfg=None, context=None)` returning the five merged-ready dicts (`flag_id/rule/clause/source/status/advisory_only/evaluation/reason/missing_inputs/decision: None/.../state`). `cfg`/`context` are injectable so tests exercise `flagged`/`clear` branches with synthetic data while the shipped config stays honest (`needs_review`).
  - **API** — new `api/readiness.py`: `GET /applications/{id}/blocked-flags` (`require_any(APPLICATION_READ, CASE_READ)`, scope via `require_application` → 404) and `POST /applications/{id}/blocked-flags/{flag_id}` (`require(CASE_TRANSITION)`; creates/updates the single decision row; audit `readiness_flag.decided` with `before={decision}`/`after={flag_id, decision, evaluation}`; `baseline_version += 1`). `_merge()` folds the decision over the evaluation (decision overrides `state`; evaluation/reason/missing inputs stay visible so the officer sees *why* after deciding). Registered in `main.py` (import + router tuple). Schemas `BlockedFlagDecision` (`pattern "^(cleared|held)$"` — the 422 guard) and `BlockedFlagOut` in `schemas/api.py`.
- Files changed: `backend/rules/readiness_policy.json`, `backend/app/services/readiness.py`, `backend/app/models/application.py`, `backend/app/models/__init__.py`, `backend/migrations/versions/0010_readiness_flags.py` (new), `backend/app/schemas/api.py`, `backend/app/api/readiness.py` (new), `backend/app/main.py`, `backend/tests/test_blocked_flags.py` (new, 31), `backend/tests/test_authz_matrix.py` (matrix + probe rows), `md/6_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` (2.3 ticked), `README.md` + `CONTEXT.md` (332 → 363, suite list, API map, 27 tables, migration list), this log.
- Migrations / API / config / rule-file changes: **migration `0010_readiness_flags`** (new table); **2 new endpoints**; new `blocked_flags` block in an existing rule file. No existing endpoint, permission constant, workflow transition or deadline behaviour changed.
- **Permission and audit effects:** GET gated by `APPLICATION_READ|CASE_READ`; POST gated by `CASE_TRANSITION` — chosen over a new permission constant deliberately: adding a permission would change the role-authority matrix, which AGENTS.md lists as a human approval gate, and PRD §7's "Regional officer clears flags" is satisfied because the regional officer already holds `CASE_TRANSITION`. **Consequence (limitation, below): scrutiny officers can also decide.** Audit: `readiness_flag.decided` written on every decision (create *and* re-decision), with before/after and the evaluation as it stood; nothing else writes audit here (unknown-flag-id 404 and permission denials follow the existing deficiency-endpoint precedent of not auditing client/scope errors).
- Decisions made: (1) **Three-valued evaluation with `clear` requiring proof** — `flagged` only when every input is recorded and matches; `clear` only when a *recorded* input provably fails a condition; anything less is `needs_review`. This lets bf-04 genuinely clear non-intake cases (sound deduction) while ensuring no unrecorded input is ever read as false (Master Prompt §2 rule 4; same precedent as absent items in sequencing). (2) **`clear` is explicitly not an official clearance** — config `evaluation_note` + tests pin that only an officer's `cleared` decision is (PRD §7); `state = decision or evaluation`. (3) **Evaluation never persisted** — only the human decision is stored (rules-as-data can evolve without stale verdicts). (4) **Clause mapping positional and labelled Unverified** — the PRD gives two parallel lists, not explicit pairs; cross-confirmations (2.16.1a, cl. 2.6) are recorded, the rest stays a labelled interpretation, never silently asserted. (5) **Placeholder input *names*** (`applicant.after_closure` etc.) are declared in config as the inputs the rule needs, each `modelled: false` with a description of what's missing — configuration with a clearly labelled placeholder (AGENTS.md), not invented data. (6) **No new permission** (see above) rather than restricting to Regional-only, which would be a role-authority change requiring approval. (7) No new DEC entry — follows DEC-0003 (advisory/unverified) and the §0 "Unverified rules never block" rule.

Initial collection was `ImportError` on `ReadinessFlag` (model genuinely absent).
